# v0.7 Parity, Docs and Release — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Take the 0.7 branch from "the memory write path works over MCP" to "a release a person can tag". That is four things. The Python binding reaches the surface the memory story depends on — the algorithms, full-text, the rule lifecycle, the memory tools as data rather than prose, and a batched property write. `find_similar`'s `min` means one thing on every surface. The README and the docs describe the tree that exists, name no other system, and state a scale claim that was measured. And the release is prepared — bumped, rehearsed, gated, with its changelog leading on what the release is for — and then stopped, one step short of the tag, with a checklist for the owner.

**Architecture:** Work that everything else stands on comes first. The defect ledger takes the findings of plans 1–3 before any of them can be lost with a scratchpad. `min` is unified through one constant in `core-api`, read by MCP, HTTP and the binding, so the three literals cannot diverge again. Then three relocations out of `crates/server` into `core-api` — `forget`, the `suggest_rules` filter with its `create_rule` arguments, the `upsert_entity` policy, and `recall` as rows — each byte-identical on the MCP side and each with tests at the layer the code now lives in, because the binding links `core-api` only and a third copy of server logic is what the doc comment on `describe_entity` in `memory/remember.rs` says this plan was supposed to avoid. Then the binding, in three tasks that each stand alone. Then the small carries. Then the documentation, in the order the claims depend on each other: names out, numbers restated, README rebuilt on what the first-run gate proves, pages corrected, two new gates so the corrections stay corrected. The changelog is written last of the prose, when everything it describes exists. The release task bumps, rehearses the publish with dry runs, runs every gate and stops.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 1.92.0 (edition 2021), cargo workspace of 10 crates; the Python binding is its **own** workspace at `bindings/python` (pyo3 0.26, `abi3-py310`, maturin ≥ 1.8), tested with pytest from `bindings/python/.venv`; Python 3 standard library for the three new gate scripts; bash ≥ 4 for the existing ones (`/opt/homebrew/bin/bash` 5.3 on this machine — stock `/bin/bash` 3.2 fails `mapfile`).

**Spec:** `docs/roadmap/v0.7-memory-write-path-spec.md` (§4.1, §4.3, §5, §6, §7, §8.4, §8.5, §9, §10, §11 tasks 12–16 and 18, §12)

**Predecessors:** plan 1 (`docs/roadmap/v0.7-memory-write-path-plan.md`, tasks 1–7) and its handoff `docs/roadmap/v0.7-plan-1-handoff.md`; plan 2 (`docs/roadmap/v0.7-code-graph-removal-plan.md`, task 8); plan 3 (`docs/roadmap/v0.7-identity-and-tools-plan.md`, tasks 9, 10, 11, 17) **plus its amendment at `4141c4e`** (alias claims, NFC), **the fix round after it, `d57e31d` and `f198839`** (`same_as_lost`), **and its second amendment, `0d959b5` through `ae4d6c6`** (derived-only `aliases`; the preset stays at sixteen rules). This plan was read at `4141c4e` and re-anchored twice, last at `ae4d6c6`; every `file:line` below is `ae4d6c6` unless it says otherwise.

> **Re-anchored at `ae4d6c6`, after plan 3's second amendment landed** (`0d959b5` through `ae4d6c6`), and revised against a pre-flight scan of this document. What the amendment changed that this plan stands on: `aliases` is derived only from a node's current key and name and is recomputed on each describing write; caller-declared aliases live only in `alias_keys`, verbatim and accumulating, and a stale name's words are never promoted into it; `forget {prop: "name"}` rewrites `aliases` in the same commit and reports `aliases_rewritten`; `remember` and `upsert_entity` report `same_as_lost`. **The identity preset is sixteen rules.** An `Entity → Entity` claim rule was held, because the engine gives an edge one owner; that defect is **ledger row 37**, deferred to 0.8, with an `#[ignore]`d engine test.
>
> **Identity code is cited by name, not by line** — `derive_aliases`, `identity_props_after_write`, `identity_props_after_forgetting_name`, `same_as_pairs`, `same_as_lost`, `identity_clusters`, `describe_entity_with_aliases`, `RememberReport` — and every other `file:line` is `ae4d6c6`'s. The rule for any that has moved by the time a task runs is the same as ever: find it by name.

| Plan | Spec tasks | State |

| **4 (this)** | **12–16, 18** | Python parity, `find_similar` unification, README and docs, the release up to but not including the tag |

**Nothing in this plan was compiled.** Plans 2 and 3 were written from a prototype built in a scratch copy of the tree. This one could not be: the copy that prototype needs is what filled the shared disk last time, and the brief forbids it. Every signature in a code block was read at `ae4d6c6` and is listed under "Self-review — verified, and how"; every expected value that is arithmetic is shown as arithmetic. What that leaves unproven is that each block compiles and each assertion holds on first run, and the plan says so where it matters: **a task's first GREEN run is its first compile**, and a step that does not go green as written is a finding to report, not a thing to force.

### The cut line

Spec §10 ranks binding parity "high value, droppable — slips to 0.7.1 cleanly" and says §6.2 "can slip"; it says §6.1, §6.3 and §7 cannot. An executor short on time drops from the bottom of the second table upward, and **never** from the first.

**The release cannot ship without these:**

| Task | Why it cannot be cut |
|---|---|
| 1 — ledger | The findings of three plans live in scratchpads that will be deleted |
| 2 — `min` | Spec §7.3, mandatory; the one silent break of the release |
| 11 — names | The standing owner rule; the front page breaks it today |
| 12 — scale | Spec §6.4; a claim the tree cannot support, on the front page |
| 13 — README | Spec §6.1; the quick start prints a flow the release replaced |
| 14 — docs | Spec §6.3; the first page a user reads teaches the commit-hunting 0.6.11 removed |
| 15 — changelog | The current title names only the removal; the release's reason for existing has no entry |
| 16 — release preparation | It is the release |

**These slip to 0.7.1 cleanly, in this order of dropping:**

| Drop | Task | What 0.7.0 then says |
|---|---|---|
| first | 10 — hook latency | The ledger row stays "unmeasured"; nothing quotes a number |
| 2nd | 9 — small carries | Four ledger rows stay open-deferred to 0.7.1 instead of closed |
| 3rd | 8 — `set_props_many` | The sidecar keeps calling `upsert_node` per node; the two `rules.md` lines move into Task 14 |
| 4th | 7 — Python memory surface | Changelog and README say the binding has the engine, not the memory tools |
| 5th | 6 — Python algorithms, full-text, rule lifecycle | The binding is what 0.6.12 shipped plus the `min` change. **Tasks 3, 4 and 5 are then cut with it**: nothing else consumes the relocations |
| with 6 and 7 | 3, 4, 5 — core-api relocations | No MCP behaviour changes either way; they exist for the binding |

Cutting a task changes two later ones and nothing else: Task 13's install table ("embed in Python" row) and Task 15's changelog section each carry an "if Task N was cut" line saying what to delete. Task 16 Step 1 re-reads both against what actually landed.

## Decisions taken on the owner's behalf

The owner pre-approved a plan built on the controller's recommendations, and on 2026-10-01 **approved PD-1 through PD-6 as defaults**. PD-1 was still being explained to the owner when this was revised, so it stays what it was written as: one isolated task. Each decision is confined to named tasks, so a different answer rewrites those tasks and nothing else.

### PD-1 — `find_similar` `min` unifies on 0.8

Today MCP defaults `min` to 0.8 (`crates/server/src/mcp.rs:852`), HTTP to 0.0 (`crates/server/src/http.rs:1961`) and Python to 0.0 (`bindings/python/src/lib.rs:511`). The spec says "unified" four times and never says to what. Ruling: **0.8 everywhere** — precision first, as O-3 chose for identity, and MCP is the primary surface. The TypeScript client has no `find_similar` and the CLI no `find-similar`, so there is no fourth site. `pairwise_similar` stays 0.0 on every surface; it already agrees with itself.

**What it costs if wrong.** This is the destructive direction. A Python or HTTP caller that never named `min` loses every hit under 0.8 — a retrieval pipeline can go from ten hits to none with no error. And `find_similar` (0.8) then disagrees with `pairwise_similar` (0.0) on every surface, where today they disagree only on MCP. If the owner prefers 0.0, MCP is the surface that moves and the failure direction is the mild one (a low-similarity tail appears). **Tasks that change:** 2 only — one constant, three test expectations, and the docs that state it; Task 15's warning inverts.

### PD-2 — the front page names no other system

The standing rule (2026-09-03): nothing committed names or compares against another project. Spec O-5 says "re-run the competitive table"; the rule wins. The README's cross-engine table becomes a table of mushroomdb's own measured numbers, each cell reproducible from a committed result file. Every tracked product-facing file is scrubbed; historical records (`docs/roadmap/**`, `benchmarks/results/**`, `docs/release-notes/**`) keep their content with the names replaced by neutral placeholders; the three adapter files whose **file names** are the names are removed and the harness becomes single-engine. A gate keeps it so.

**PD-2a, the sub-decision: how the gate knows the names without committing them.** The gate is `scripts/check-names.py`, and its list is `scripts/blocked-names.sha256` — one SHA-256 digest per line, of each name lowercased. The script hashes the words of every tracked file and fails on a match. Nothing tracked spells a name, the gate needs no exemption for its own list, and CI can run it. The cost: it is security by inconvenience, not secrecy (a dictionary recovers the list), and adding a name is a command, not an edit. The alternative — the names in plain text in the script, exempted from its own scan — is simpler and commits the names. **Tasks that change if the owner prefers plain text:** 11 Step 2 only.

**PD-2b: the relational baseline is reworded, not gated.** The association benchmark's baseline arm is a relational file, and the README table heading and two sentences name the engine it uses (`README.md:409`, `:417`). That engine's name is also a Python standard-library module the harness imports, so it cannot go on the gate's list. Task 11 rewords the product-facing mentions to "the relational baseline" and leaves the harness alone.

**What it costs if wrong.** If the owner meant to exempt engine benchmarks, the tree loses a comparison it had evidence for, three adapters and the test classes that covered their skip paths. Git history keeps all of it; restoring is a revert of Task 11's two commits. **Tasks that change:** 11, and the benchmark section of 13.

### PD-3 — `explain` and `rename_node` stay served and unlisted

No listing change. Spec task 13's "move the tool listing" was completed by plan 2 (correction S2 below), and spec §12's question has been passed along twice. Listing either moves all fifteen pins of plan 3's Procedure L, the skill's byte budget (259 bytes left) and the listing's byte count. **What it costs if wrong:** an agent still cannot reach `rename_node` without `--all-tools`. **Tasks that change:** none here; it would be a new task shaped like plan 3's Tasks 4–7.

### PD-4 — the scale claim is restated to what was measured; no new large run

Nothing above 100,000 nodes has ever been run, and the 12.73 GiB "ceiling" the spec quotes belongs to a reopen path deleted in v0.2. The claim is restated in all six places. **What it costs if wrong:** the README stops saying "10M". If the owner wants a measured larger point instead, it is a new measurement task (a release build of the binding, a 1M-node synthetic store, about an hour of wall clock), and Task 12's sentences gain a row. **Tasks that change:** 12.

### PD-5 — the rendered docs site slips to 0.7.1

Spec §10 allows it. Whether Pages is enabled at all is unverified (`docs/site/launch-checklist.md:9-12` has it unchecked). This plan corrects the pages and unpublishes the internal notes; it adds no site generator. **What it costs if wrong:** `pages.yml` keeps serving raw Markdown. **Tasks that change:** a new one; none here.

### PD-6 — the release stops before the tag

Pushing a `v*` tag publishes to four registries and a GitHub Release, and eight of those publications can never be withdrawn. Task 16 bumps, rehearses, gates and ends with a checklist. **What it costs if wrong:** nothing but a hand-off.

## Corrections to the spec, made by this plan

Each was verified at `ae4d6c6`. The spec is closed for planning; Task 15 Step 5 amends it in place, in the blockquote style of its existing corrections.

### S1 — every README line number in §6.1 and §6.4 is stale, and a third of §6.1 is done

The spec was written against a 537-line README; it is 497 now. `## Deprecations` (spec `:174-199`) is gone — plan 2 deleted it with the seven tool rows and seven CLI rows. `## Agent memory` (spec `:200`) is at `README.md:173`. The install list (spec `:295-301`) is `README.md:263-269`. The scale claim (spec `:388`) is `README.md:348`. The table (spec `:402`) is `README.md:361-398`. `crates/cli/src/main.rs:773-798` (the silent API-only fallback) is `:673-697`. **Tasks 13 and 14 find sections by heading, never by the spec's numbers.**

### S2 — task 13's "move the tool listing" is already done

Plan 2 Task 6 (`82fc6f3`) deleted `CODE_GRAPH_TOOLS`, `Surface` and `surface_of`. `ASSOCIATION_TOOLS` is one list of 23 (`crates/server/src/mcp.rs:1262`), served to every store; `crates/server/tests/mcp.rs:1521` and `:1550` pin it and `CHANGELOG.md:11-14` says it. Nothing is left to move. This plan verifies it (Task 16 Step 4) and changes nothing.

### S3 — §7.1's pin defect is already fixed

Plan 1 (`93ef846`): `bindings/python/Cargo.toml:18-19` pin `0.6.12`; `scripts/bump-version.sh:92` enumerates `bindings/*/Cargo.toml`; `scripts/tests/test-bump-version-scope.sh` runs in CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:401`). The spec's `bump-version.sh:97` is `:92`. **Two blind spots remain that the spec does not name**, and Task 16 closes both: `docs/site/index.md:57` ("v0.6.12 is the current release") matches no pattern the script or `check-claims.sh` scans, and `bump-version.sh:145` swallows a failed lockfile refresh for the binding (`|| true`).

### S4 — "MCP (0.8) and Python (0.0)" is a two-surface description of a three-surface problem

HTTP `POST /find_similar` defaults to 0.0 too (`crates/server/src/http.rs:1961`, doc `:1939`) and has its own test pinning it (`crates/server/tests/http.rs:1013`). And the spec never says which value wins while §9 says no decision is open. PD-1.

### S5 — §6.4's "measured ceiling of 12.73 GiB" is not a ceiling of anything that ships

`dogfood/results/scale-100k.md:38`: 12.73 GiB is the lifetime peak of `reopen_snap` on v0.1.1 — the V6 decompress-into-heap reopen. That path was deleted in v0.2; the same store opens from a V8 snapshot in 0.02 s at 31–41 MiB (`:184-211`). Quoting 12.73 GiB as the ceiling replaces one stale claim with another. The build-time peak, 4.72 GiB (`:29-37`), is the number that still describes something. The claim also lives in six places, not the spec's two (Task 12).

### S6 — O-5's re-run cannot be executed inside the owner's rule

A re-run produces a committed results file titled with three other systems' names and refreshes a front-page table whose heading names them. PD-2. O-5's intent — the number on the front page is current — is kept: Task 11 re-measures mushroomdb's own column on the 0.7 tree.

### S7 — the requested acceptance for `set_props_many` contradicts itself

The request: "`wal_total_commits()` advances by exactly 1 … and rules fire on the changed props in that commit". `wal_total_commits` counts WAL **frames** (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs:12394`), and a commit whose rules change any edge appends one more, of history markers (`db.rs:5409-5445`, "it occupies a frame that every history surface counts"). Probed on the installed 0.6.11 wheel: a watched field whose change derives or retracts an edge moves the counter by **2**. Restated (controller R2): exactly one user commit; plus at most one marker frame, when an edge is derived or retracted; 0 on an identical repeat. Task 8 asserts each case separately.

### S8 — plan 3's two claims about the binding are both off

"It has no upsert at all" (plan 3 `:5960`): it has `upsert_node` (`bindings/python/src/lib.rs:205`). What it lacks is the *memory* upsert — alias maintenance, clearing `provisional`, the relabel refusal. "The predicate shape differs … cannot be assumed to round-trip": the binding accepts MCP's serde shape verbatim (`rule_from_py`, `lib.rs:2320-2339`; `normalize_predicate` passes anything without a string `kind` through). The real divergence is `weight_prop`: MCP defaults a missing one to `"weight"` (`crates/server/src/json.rs:315-317`), Python leaves it `None`. `create_rule_args` writes it out explicitly, so its output creates the same rule on both — which is why Task 4 moves that function rather than letting the binding serialise the raw definition.

### S9 — `docs/design.md` cannot be fixed by the three rows §6.3 lists

It is a pre-implementation spec dated 2026-08-14: "name TBD", "Apache 2.0", "Git repo not yet initialized", an empty `## 12. Next Step`, eleven pointers into a gitignored directory, four named systems in a market rationale, and a cross-binding conformance corpus that was never built. Patching lines 56, 63 and 234 leaves a document that says the repository does not exist. Task 14 marks it as the historical record it is and stops linking it as "the full design spec" (the smaller honest option; the argument is in that task).

### S10 — three counts in §6.2 have moved

`docs/site` holds 26 `.md` files, not 27 (plan 2 deleted `code-graph.md`). `index.html` has five `href`s, not six. `index.md` links 11 pages, not 12.

### S11 — the first install command in every doc does not exist

`claude marketplace add MatthewSherlin/mushroomdb` appears in ten tracked files. On the CLI installed on this machine `marketplace` is a subcommand of `plugin` — `claude plugin marketplace add <source>` (`claude plugin marketplace --help`, read 2026-10-01); the root command has no `marketplace`, so the documented line is parsed as a prompt. The spec calls install-path presentation "the single largest observed onboarding friction" and did not notice. Task 13 re-verifies against the installed CLI's help, then fixes all seven hand-written sites in one commit with a gate.

### S12 — `docs/site/api.md` says "Seventeen tools" and is outside every task's file list

`docs/site/api.md:1019`, then a 17-row table. The server serves 25. The table omits eight tools, calls `explain_association` an "Alias of `explain`" (`:1034`), and gives `min` two defaults in one cell (`:1032`). Plan 3's count sweep did not touch it. Task 14.

### S13 — the changelog is not a loud line away from done

`CHANGELOG.md:3` is titled "the code graph door is closed". Plan 1 never wrote an entry: the release's reason for existing appears only as an aside at `:69-70`. `find_similar` and `min` are not mentioned anywhere in the 0.7.0 section (`:3-293`). Task 15 rewrites the head of the section.

### S14 — task 18 writes "tag" as if it were a step

PD-6.

## Deviations from the controller's rulings

Two, both on reading the code the ruling was about. Each is small and each is reversible.

### DV-1 — the `analyze` shaping is not relocated

R1 lists "the `analyze` shaping" among what moves from `crates/server` into `core-api`, under the rule "anything private to `crates/server` that both MCP and Python need". On reading `tool_analyze` (`crates/server/src/mcp_tasks.rs:2754` on), Python needs none of it. The shaping is a top-N cut at 50 rows, a five-key sample, keys cut at 80 characters, scores as fixed-width strings and a rendered header — presentation sized for an assistant's context window. A program wants the rows, and the four engine calls return them whole (`GraphDb::pagerank` and its three siblings, `crates/core-api/src/db.rs:8079-8138`). The one thing the binding takes from it is the lesson, not the code: `budget_ms` defaults to `0`, so the same store gives the same answer. Moving roughly 210 lines byte-identically for no consumer is risk with no purchaser.

**If the controller wants it moved anyway** it is one more relocation of exactly Task 3's shape — `core_api::memory::analyze::analyze(db, kind, top, edge_type) -> Result<(String, serde_json::Value), String>`, the server keeping argument parsing and `unknown_edge_type` — inserted after Task 5. Nothing else in this plan changes.

### DV-2 — R6's disk figure is out of date, and the threshold is set against today's

R6 says about 9 GB is free. Measured while this plan was written, after the build cache was cleared: **48 GiB free** (`df -h /`). Procedure P keeps R6's discipline — free regenerable output first, measure, stop if short — with a threshold of 15 GiB, which is what a from-scratch debug build of the root workspace (measured at 48 GB when full of every profile and feature set; one debug profile is a fraction of it) and the binding's second tree (5.2 GB) need between them with room for the other agents.

## Global Constraints

Copied from plan 3 and updated; every task's requirements implicitly include these.

- **Rust 1.92.0** (`rust-toolchain.toml`, `workspace.package.rust-version`). Do not raise it.
- **No new WAL discriminant. No storage format change.** Snapshot versions 5–10 stay readable. A 0.6.x store must open in 0.7.
- **No new dependency.** The binding already depends on `serde_json` (`bindings/python/Cargo.toml:21`) and `core-api` on `serde` and `serde_json`; nothing in this plan needs more. The binding does **not** gain a dependency on `mushroomdb-server` — that would pull axum and tokio into the wheel.
- **`enable_fulltext` is not idempotent** (`db.rs:7346-7354`). In core code always go through `apply_schema`; in the binding it is exposed with `if_not_exists=`.
- **Existing stores are never silently schema-upgraded. A rule is never created silently.**
- **A relocation is byte-identical on the MCP side.** The server's tests for the moved behaviour are the pin: they are not edited in the commit that moves the code. New tests go where the code now lives.
- **Determinism.** Collections sorted, ties broken on the key ascending, floats at fixed precision where they are printed, no wall-clock budget in an answer that claims to be repeatable.
- **Every rendered line is sanitized** with `core_api::digest::sanitize`.
- **The binding's lock order (defect #17).** `graph_err` takes the GIL (`bindings/python/src/lib.rs:2000`). Every access helper drops the store mutex **before** mapping an error (`:1772-1773`, `:1805-1806`, `:1868-1869`). A new method never calls `lock()` directly and never maps an error under the guard: it goes through `with_mut`, `with_ref` or `with_scope`.
- **`abi3-py310`.** Dicts, lists, tuples, floats, ints, strings, bools, `None`. Nothing outside the limited API.
- **Every public method on the binding owes five things**, and `test_every_method_has_a_row` fails the suite on the fourth: a `///` doc comment and a `text_signature`; a `def` with a docstring in `bindings/python/mushroomdb.pyi`; a name in `_DOCUMENTED` (`bindings/python/tests/test_parity.py:483`); a row in `COVERED` (`bindings/python/tests/test_scope_leaks.py:675`) naming a real test in that file; and a line in `bindings/python/README.md`.
- **Nothing committed names another system.** Not in code, comments, docs, test fixtures, commit messages or this plan. Refer to "the named systems" and to files by path.
- **Verification bar, per commit that touches Rust:**

  ```bash
  cargo fmt --all -- --check
  cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
  cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings -A clippy::too_many_arguments
  cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast 2>&1 | grep -E "^test result: " | awk '{p+=$4; f+=$6; i+=$8} END {print NR" binaries, "p" passed, "f" failed, "i" ignored"}'
  ```

  The `-A clippy::too_many_arguments` is for the pre-existing `crates/server/src/http.rs:484`. **Count only `^test result: ` lines.** **`--no-fail-fast` is not optional**, and never read the exit code of a pipeline through `tail` or `grep`. **A build, a test run or a gate that feeds an `&&` chain is either not piped at all or runs under `set -o pipefail`**, which every multi-step invocation below sets first — a failed `maturin develop` piped through `tail` would otherwise let pytest run against the stale wheel and report green. Run long commands in the foreground.
- **Rust baseline: the head after plan 3's second amendment; the executor re-measures.** The controller's figure for `ae4d6c6` is **125 test binaries, 2,555 passed, 0 failed, 33 ignored**; this plan measured nothing. **Task 1 Step 1 measures the tree it starts from and records it; every later task states a delta against the count the task before it left**, never an absolute.
- **Python baseline.** None is on record: the wheel installed in `bindings/python/.venv` is `mushroomdb-0.6.11`, two releases stale, so no pytest result against it means anything. Procedure P's first run rebuilds it and records the count, before any binding edit.
- **The binding is not in the root workspace.** `cargo test --workspace` and both clippy runs do not compile `bindings/python`. A change to a `core-api` signature the binding uses is caught only by Procedure P. Tasks 3, 4 and 5 therefore end with it.
- **Commit style:** conventional, lowercase, short. **No `Co-Authored-By`, no session trailers, ever.**
- **Stage explicitly.** Never `git add -A` or `git add .`: this tree carries unrelated untracked files (`benchmarks/longmemeval/`, `mushroom-memory/`, three `benchmarks/agent-tasks/results/2026090*` directories, four roadmap drafts, two `.txt` drafts). Every commit step names its paths.
- **Never run a git action beyond the commits this plan specifies.** No push, no tag, no branch, no merge. No `cargo publish` without `--dry-run`, no `npm publish` without `--dry-run`, no `maturin publish`, no `gh release`, no `gh workflow run`.
- **Disk (R6).** No `git archive` copy, no worktree, no extra target directory. Build in place. Procedure P's first step is the only place build output is deleted, and it deletes nothing else.
- **An agent's shell keeps no variables and no working directory between calls.** A sequence that needs either is one invocation; every path is absolute or relative to the repository root.
- **`mushroomdb query` `CREATE` needs an `id:` property** — it is the node's key.
- **Verify every signature before you use it.** Every `file:line` here was read at `ae4d6c6`; this plan's own earlier tasks move some of them, so re-read before editing.
- **A test that cannot fail is not a test.** Every new test says what RED looks like; run it RED before writing the code it guards, and paste the failure into your report. A new gate script is shown failing on a fixture before it is trusted.
- **A measurement is written down before it is run.** What is measured, how, on what, and what will be reported — committed before the number exists. Tasks 10 and 11 each do this.

## Review Focus

Five classes of input the spec implies and that no task's own happy-path test would exercise. Each has a test, in the task that owns the behaviour; a reviewer should check these five first.

| # | Input class | Why it is easy to miss | Test | Task |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RF-1 | **A scoped handle calling a whole-store read** | None of the new reads takes a mask — no algorithm, no full-text search, no recall, no suggestion. A method that simply works on a `scoped()` handle is a leak that returns a correct-looking answer. `test_every_method_has_a_row` forces a row; it cannot force the row to be a refusal. | `test_a_whole_store_read_is_refused_on_a_scoped_handle` (parametrised over all eleven) and `test_a_keyed_write_refuses_identically_for_hidden_and_absent` rows for `set_props_many`, `upsert_entity`, `remember`, `forget` | 6, 7, 8 |
| RF-2 | **A `set_props_many` row that queues no operation** | The engine checks a key only when an operation names it (`db.rs:6525`). A row whose dict is empty, or whose every value is unchanged, queues nothing — so an unknown key in such a row would pass silently, and "an unknown key is an error" would hold only for keys that happen to change. | `test_an_unknown_key_is_an_error_even_when_its_row_changes_nothing` | 8 |
| RF-3 | **A watched property in `set_props_many`** | The frame counter moves by 2, not 1, when a rule derives or retracts an edge. A test that changes only an unwatched integer passes an implementation that never fires rules at all. | `test_rules_fire_in_the_same_commit_and_cost_one_marker_frame` | 8 |
| RF-4 | **A caller that omits `min`, on each surface, at a similarity between 0.0 and 0.8** | Every in-repo caller but three passes `min` explicitly. A test at similarity 1.0 passes under either default; one at 0.0 cannot tell 0.8 from 0.5. The fixture needs a hit *between* the two defaults. | `http_find_similar_defaults_min_to_the_shared_constant`, `test_find_similar_default_min_is_0_8`, and the extended `test_find_similar_vector_default_min_is_0_8` — each with a 0.6-similarity node | 2 |
| RF-5 | **A name that is not a word of its own** | The names gate tokenises on letters and digits. A name glued to a suffix — an identifier, a file name, a driver package — is one longer token and passes a whole-word check. The three adapter files were exactly this. | `test-check-names.sh`: a fixture whose only hit is `<name>store.py` as a **path** and `<name>Graph` inside an identifier | 11 |

## Procedure P — building and testing the binding

Tasks 2 through 8 each end with this. It is one invocation because an agent's shell keeps nothing between calls.

**P1 — free regenerable output, measure, and stop if short (R6).** Only build output is touched: the two `release` profile directories, which nothing in Tasks 2–9 needs.

```bash
set -o pipefail; cd /Users/nilrehsttam/Repos/graph-db \
  && cargo clean --release \
  && (cd bindings/python && cargo clean --release) \
  && df -g / | awk 'NR==2 {print "free_gib=" $4; if ($4 < 15) {print "BLOCKED: under 15 GiB free"; exit 1}}'
```

Expected: `free_gib=<n>` with `n ≥ 15`. If it prints `BLOCKED`, stop and report **BLOCKED** with the figure. Do not delete anything else to get under the line — not `target/debug`, not another agent's files, nothing outside build output.

**P2 — build the wheel into the venv and run the binding's tests.** `set -o pipefail` is the first word for a reason: Tasks 3, 4 and 5 run this only to prove the binding still compiles, and without it a failed build would be followed by a passing test run on the previous wheel.

```bash
set -o pipefail; cd /Users/nilrehsttam/Repos/graph-db/bindings/python \
  && .venv/bin/maturin develop 2>&1 | tail -3 \
  && .venv/bin/python -c "import mushroomdb, importlib.metadata as m; print('installed', m.version('mushroomdb'))" \
  && .venv/bin/pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -3
```

Expected: `installed 0.6.12` until Task 16 bumps it, then `installed 0.7.0`; pytest's last line `N passed` with no `failed` and no `error`. **If `.venv` does not exist**, create it exactly as CI does (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:246-247`): `python3 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install -U pip maturin pytest`.

**P3 — the stub and claims gates**, which read `lib.rs` and the `.pyi` as text:

```bash
set -o pipefail; cd /Users/nilrehsttam/Repos/graph-db && bash scripts/check-pyi.sh && bash scripts/check-claims.sh
```

Expected: `check-pyi.sh: OK` and `check-claims.sh: OK`.

**The first time anyone runs P2** — Task 2 Step 1 — it is run on the untouched tree, and the `N passed` it prints is the Python baseline. Record it. Every later task states how many tests it adds.

```markdown

---

# Found in 0.7 (rows 38–63)

Entered 2026-10-01 by plan 4, Task 1. Until then these lived in session ledgers that are not in
the repository. Sources: plan 2's final review and fix report, plan 3's ledger and final review,
`docs/roadmap/v0.7-plan-1-handoff.md`, and the research for plan 4.

## 38. Hook latency on a large store is unmeasured — DEFERRED

Plan 2's "Known costs" (`docs/roadmap/v0.7-code-graph-removal-plan.md:2154`): about 0.24 s per
prompt on a store ingested from this repository, release build; "only a 10k-node store was
measured". The hook times out at 5 s. Closes with a recorded number on a 100,000-note store.

## 39. A CLI-only install's empty brief names tools it cannot call — DEFERRED

`EMPTY_BRIEF` offers `remember`, `upsert_entity` and `ingest_json`. A `--delivery cli` install
registers no MCP server. `reach_line` already branches on delivery; the empty line does not.

## 40. Evidence lines have no byte cap — DEFERRED

Plan 2 final review, M8: one evidence line printed fifty values, about 2 KB, and `why` prints
each direction. `cap_lines` bounds the count of lines, not their length.

## 41. `render_schema` has no total byte cap — DEFERRED

Plan 3 ledger, Task 4. Sections are capped at 20 lines, 12 properties per label and 10
provisional keys, so only long names grow it. The `json: true` report is uncapped deliberately:
a program asked for the whole report.

## 42. `forget` names rules by edge type and labels, not by provenance — DEFERRED

Plan 3 ledger, Task 5. Two rules deriving the same edge type between the same labels are both
named when one derived the edge. `GraphDb::explain` knows which.

## 43. A whitespace-padded key gained one canonicalised alias — CLOSED

Plan 3 ledger, final "parked" line. While `aliases` accumulated, the alias of a padded key was
the key lowercased and not trimmed, so the next write folded it and added its canonical form
beside it, once. Plan 3's second amendment (`0d959b5`) made `aliases` a function of the current
key and name, recomputed on each describing write. Nothing accumulates, so there is no second
copy to gain.

## 44. Five retired hook subcommands still parse — DEFERRED

Plan 2 final review, ruling I3. A machine that upgraded without re-running `install` still
calls them from a hook; each prints how to remove it. Delete in 0.8.

## 45. A rule created alone broke every lock-free reader — CLOSED

Plan 3, correction S8 and Task 1. `60b3e57`.

## 46. The ignored-test census cannot fail — DEFERRED

`docs/roadmap/v0.7-plan-1-handoff.md:299-302`. 32 ignored, 9 named in a workflow, 23 never run
(`bash scripts/ignored-test-census.sh` at `ae4d6c6`; run it again and write what it prints).

## 47. `ingest` and `insert_node` were never compared — DEFERRED

`docs/roadmap/v0.7-plan-1-handoff.md:290-294`: "Worth one explicit check before release." No
later ledger records that check. Listed in plan 4 under "Owner decisions still open".

## 48. `matched` undercounts existing fact endpoints — DEFERRED

`docs/roadmap/v0.7-plan-1-handoff.md:303-304`.

## 49. The ledger gate ran only from the repository root — CLOSED

`LEDGER` was a bare relative path. Shown failing from `/tmp`, then fixed, in plan 4 Task 1.

## 50. `forget` under-reports edges through a via node — DEFERRED

Plan 3 final review, minor.

## 51. `same_as_pairs` swallows read errors — DEFERRED

Plan 3 final review, minor. `let Ok(edges) = db.node_edges(key) else { continue }`.

## 52. A suggestion can carry `approximate: true` — DEFERRED

Plan 3 final review, minor. `create_rule` accepts it; its advertised schema does not list it.

## 53. A stub's aliases are not capped — DEFERRED

Plan 3 final review, minor.

## 54. All-features clippy is red and CI does not run it — DEFERRED

Pre-existing. `crates/server/src/http.rs:484`.

## 55. The store-creation census sees only the CLI — DEFERRED

Plan 2 final review. A store created through the Python binding or by the HTTP server on a
fresh path gets no schema, and the census that guards "every path that creates a store declares
a schema" cannot see either.

## 56. Three blind spots in `check-claims.sh` — DEFERRED

Plan 2 ledger, Task 10, and the research for plan 4.

## 57. `load_tasks` defaults to a retired suite — DEFERRED

Plan 2 ledger, Task 10. Unreachable behind the guard; wrong if the guard moves.
```

### Task 2: `find_similar` means one thing — `min` is 0.8 on every surface (PD-1)

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/core-api/src/lib.rs` (the constant, after the `pub use exact_knn::…` line at `:43`)
- Modify: `crates/server/src/mcp.rs:852` (the handler), `:1492` (the schema description), `:2406-2461` (the inline test)
- Modify: `crates/server/src/http.rs:1939` (doc), `:1961` (the default)
- Modify: `crates/server/tests/http.rs:1010-1046` (one test amended, one added)
- Modify: `bindings/python/src/lib.rs:479-513` (doc comment, `signature`, `text_signature`)
- Modify: `bindings/python/mushroomdb.pyi:634`, `:671-675`
- Modify: `bindings/python/tests/test_exact_reads.py` (one test added)
- Modify: `bindings/python/README.md:121-124`; `docs/site/api.md:686-687`, `:1032`, `:1285`, `:1298-1299`; `docs/site/mcp.md:175-179`, `:480-481`, `:490`; `CHANGELOG.md:196-197` (the byte counts only — the warning itself is Task 15's)
- Regenerate: `llms-full.txt`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: `pub const FIND_SIMILAR_DEFAULT_MIN: f64 = 0.8;` at the root of `core_api`. Task 15's changelog warning and Task 13's README quote the value; Task 16's listing measurement expects the byte counts this task leaves.

**Why:** Spec §7.3, mandatory; PD-1; correction S4. Three surfaces hard-code three literals (`mcp.rs:852`, `http.rs:1961`, `lib.rs:511`) and that is how they diverged — the engine takes `min` as a required argument (`crates/core-api/src/db.rs:10102`) and has no opinion. One constant, read by all three, is the fix that stays fixed.

**Who this breaks, said once here and loudly in Task 15.** A Python caller of `find_similar` that omits `min`, and an HTTP caller of `POST /find_similar` that omits `min`, lose every hit under 0.8. Nothing raises. MCP callers see no change. `pairwise_similar` is untouched: 0.0 on every surface, as before.

**The fixture (RF-4).** Three two-dimensional vectors against the query `[1.0, 0.0]`:

| key | vector | cosine with the query |
|---|---|---|
| `close` | `[1.0, 0.0]` | 1.0 |
| `mid` | `[0.6, 0.8]` | 0.6 — `0.6 / (1.0 × √(0.36 + 0.64))` |
| `far` | `[0.0, 1.0]` | 0.0 |

`mid` is the point of it. It sits between the two defaults, so a default of 0.8 returns `close` alone and a default of 0.0 returns all three. The existing tests use only `close` and `far`, which cannot tell 0.8 from 0.5.

- [ ] **Step 1: Take the Python baseline**

Run Procedure P (P1, P2, P3) on the untouched tree. Record the `N passed` line as `PYBASE`. This is the first build of the binding since 0.6.11 and takes several minutes.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the HTTP tests**

In `crates/server/tests/http.rs`, the existing test at `:1013` sends no `min` and expects the orthogonal hit. It is a shape test — HTTP hits equal the engine's, as `[[key, score], …]` — and stays one: give it the `min` it has been relying on. Replace its doc comment (`:1010-1011`) and add `"min": 0.0` to its request body:

```rust
/// Binding: POST /find_similar exact hits equal GraphDb::find_similar_vector_filtered
/// on the same store (Python tuple shape: [[key, score], ...]). `min` is named,
/// because the default no longer keeps the orthogonal hit.
#[tokio::test]
async fn http_find_similar_exact_agrees_with_python_shape() {
    let (app, db) = open("http-find-similar-exact");
    seed_emb(&db, "Item", "close", 1.0, 0.0);
    seed_emb(&db, "Item", "far", 0.0, 1.0);

    let expected = {
        let g = db.read();
        g.find_similar_vector_filtered("emb", Some("Item"), &[1.0, 0.0], 10, 0.0, None, None, true)
            .unwrap()
    };
    assert!(
        expected.iter().any(|(k, _)| k == "far"),
        "engine min=0.0 must keep the orthogonal hit: {expected:?}"
    );

    let (status, body, _) = send(
        app,
        json_req(
            "POST",
            "/find_similar",
            json!({
                "field": "emb",
                "vector": [1.0, 0.0],
                "k": 10,
                "min": 0.0,
                "label": "Item",
                "exact": true
            }),
        ),
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, StatusCode::OK, "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&body));
    let v = parse_json(&body);
    assert_eq!(v["hits"], serde_json::to_value(&expected).unwrap());
}
```

Directly after it, add the test that pins the default:

```rust
/// Binding (plan 4, PD-1): a `POST /find_similar` that names no `min` applies
/// `core_api::FIND_SIMILAR_DEFAULT_MIN`, the same floor MCP and the Python
/// binding apply. `mid` scores 0.6 — between the old HTTP default (0.0) and
/// the unified one (0.8) — so it is the hit that tells them apart.
#[tokio::test]
async fn http_find_similar_defaults_min_to_the_shared_constant() {
    let (app, db) = open("http-find-similar-default-min");
    seed_emb(&db, "Item", "close", 1.0, 0.0);
    seed_emb(&db, "Item", "mid", 0.6, 0.8);
    seed_emb(&db, "Item", "far", 0.0, 1.0);

    let keys_for = |v: &Json| -> Vec<String> {
        v["hits"]
            .as_array()
            .expect("hits")
            .iter()
            .map(|h| h[0].as_str().expect("key").to_string())
            .collect()
    };
    let body_without_min = json!({
        "field": "emb", "vector": [1.0, 0.0], "k": 10, "label": "Item", "exact": true
    });

    let (status, body, _) = send(
        app.clone(),
        json_req("POST", "/find_similar", body_without_min),
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, StatusCode::OK, "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&body));
    assert_eq!(
        keys_for(&parse_json(&body)),
        vec!["close"],
        "no min named: only the hit at or above {} survives",
        core_api::FIND_SIMILAR_DEFAULT_MIN
    );

    // Naming it still reaches everything, in score order.
    let (status, body, _) = send(
        app,
        json_req(
            "POST",
            "/find_similar",
            json!({
                "field": "emb", "vector": [1.0, 0.0], "k": 10, "min": 0.0,
                "label": "Item", "exact": true
            }),
        ),
    )
    .await;
    assert_eq!(status, StatusCode::OK, "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&body));
    assert_eq!(keys_for(&parse_json(&body)), vec!["close", "mid", "far"]);
}
```

Verify before running: `Json` is the alias this file uses for `serde_json::Value` (`json_req`'s third parameter, `:48`), `open` returns `(Router, SharedDb)` (`:28`), and `axum::Router` is `Clone`. If the file's alias has a different name, use it.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the Python test**

Append to `bindings/python/tests/test_exact_reads.py`:

```python
def test_find_similar_default_min_is_0_8(tmp_path):
    """Since 0.7 `min` defaults to 0.8 here, as it does over MCP and HTTP.

    `mid` scores 0.6 against the query — between the old default (0.0) and
    the new one — so it is the hit that tells the two apart. Before 0.7 the
    first call returned all three.
    """
    db = GraphDb.open(str(tmp_path / "db"))
    db.insert_node("Item", "close", {"emb": [1.0, 0.0]})
    db.insert_node("Item", "mid", {"emb": [0.6, 0.8]})
    db.insert_node("Item", "far", {"emb": [0.0, 1.0]})

    default = db.find_similar("emb", [1.0, 0.0], label="Item", k=10, exact=True)
    assert [k for k, _ in default] == ["close"]

    named = db.find_similar("emb", [1.0, 0.0], label="Item", k=10, min=0.0, exact=True)
    assert [k for k, _ in named] == ["close", "mid", "far"]

    assert "min=0.8" in GraphDb.find_similar.__text_signature__
    # pairwise_similar is a different operation and keeps its own default.
    assert "min=0.0" in GraphDb.pairwise_similar.__text_signature__
    db.close()
```

```rust
/// The similarity floor `find_similar` applies when the caller names none.
///
/// One value, read by every surface that has a default — the MCP tool, HTTP
```

### Task 3: `forget` lives in `core-api` (relocation, R1)

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/core-api/src/memory/forget.rs`
- Create: `crates/core-api/tests/memory_forget.rs`
- Modify: `crates/core-api/src/memory/mod.rs` (`pub mod forget;`, in alphabetical position after `brief`)
- Modify: `crates/server/src/mcp_tasks.rs` — delete `FORGET_NOTE_LIST` (`:2100`), `FORGET_SHAPE` (`:2103`), `ForgetReport` (`:2108`), `predicate_fields` (`:2138`), `notes_about` (`:2147`), `EdgeTriple` and `derived_edges_on` (`:2165-2177`), `rule_owned_refusal` (`:2419`, to the end of the function); rewrite `tool_forget` (`:2274-2415`); keep `history_line`, `ALIASES_REWRITTEN_LINE`, `alias_keys_remain_line`, `render_forget`, `fact_arg`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from this plan.
- Produces, in `core_api::memory::forget`:
  - `pub const FORGET_NOTE_LIST: usize = 10;`
  - `pub const FORGET_SHAPE: &str`
  - `pub enum ForgetTarget { Node { key: String }, Prop { key: String, prop: String }, Fact { subject: String, predicate: String, object: String } }` with `pub fn from_parts(key: Option<String>, prop: Option<String>, fact: Option<(String, String, String)>) -> Option<ForgetTarget>`
  - `pub struct ForgetReport` — `Serialize`, every field `pub`, same names and order as the server's
  - `pub fn forget<F: Fs>(db: &mut GraphDb<F>, target: &ForgetTarget) -> Result<ForgetReport>` — a rule-derived fact is `Err(GraphError::RuleOwned { detail })` whose `detail` is the whole refusal sentence
  - `pub fn rule_owned_refusal<F: Fs>(db: &GraphDb<F>, predicate: &str, subject: &str, object: &str, detail: &str) -> String`
  - `pub fn predicate_fields(p: &PredicateSummary) -> Vec<String>`
  Task 4 imports `predicate_fields`; Task 7 calls `forget` from the binding; Task 9 changes `rule_owned_refusal`'s body.

**Why:** Controller R1. The binding links `core-api` only (`bindings/python/Cargo.toml:18-19`), and `tool_forget` is about 120 lines of policy — which notes still say it, which derived edges a property removal retracted, what the refusal for a rule-owned edge names — that a Python `forget` would otherwise have to copy. What moves is the decision and the report. What stays is the rendering: `render_forget`, with `history_line`, the `ALIASES_REWRITTEN_LINE` sentence and `alias_keys_remain_line`, is how an MCP reply reads, and the binding returns the report as a dict.

**The byte-identity argument, path by path.** The server's fourteen `forget` tests in `crates/server/tests/mcp.rs` (`:5249` through `:5579`, and `:6188`, `:6230`, `:6287`) are the pin and are not edited. Two of them hold the amended property arm in place: `forgetting_a_name_takes_its_words_out_of_aliases_and_says_so` (`:5315`) and `forgetting_a_name_retracts_the_link_it_made` (`:5392`).

| Path | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| success | `ok(json_out, &ForgetReport, render_forget)` | the same call on `core_api`'s `ForgetReport` — same field names, same order, same `skip_serializing_if` on `prop` |
| not one of the three shapes | `ToolErr(FORGET_SHAPE)` | `ForgetTarget::from_parts` returns `None` → `ToolErr(FORGET_SHAPE)` |
| unknown key, or any engine error | `ToolErr(graph_err_msg(e))` | `forget` returns `Err(e)` → `ToolErr(graph_err_msg(e))` |
| rule-derived fact | `ToolErr(rule_owned_refusal(…))` | `forget` returns `Err(RuleOwned { detail: rule_owned_refusal(…) })` → `ToolErr(detail)`, **only when the target is a fact** |

The guard on the last row matters. `graph_err_msg` (`crates/server/src/mcp.rs:1205`) renders a `RuleOwned` through `Display`, prefix and all. If a `RuleOwned` ever came out of the node or property arm it went through `graph_err_msg` before, so it must still.

Create `crates/core-api/tests/memory_forget.rs`:

```rust
//! `forget` at the layer it lives in: a node, one property or one fact, and a
//! report of what is left behind. The MCP tool renders this report; the
//! Python binding returns it. Moved out of `crates/server` in 0.7.
use core_api::memory::forget::{forget, ForgetTarget, FORGET_NOTE_LIST};
use core_api::memory::remember::{remember, RememberInput};
use core_api::memory_schema::memory_defaults;
use core_api::{Direction, GraphDb, GraphError, Predicate, RuleDef, Value};

type Db = GraphDb<core_storage::fs::RealFs>;

fn tmp(name: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
    let nanos = std::time::SystemTime::now()
        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
        .unwrap()
        .as_nanos();
    std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
        "mdb-memforget-{name}-{}-{nanos}",
        std::process::id()
    ))
}

fn store(name: &str) -> Db {
    let mut db = GraphDb::open(&tmp(name)).unwrap();
    db.apply_schema(&memory_defaults()).unwrap();
    db
}

/// One note about `about`, returning the note's key. `ts` makes each distinct.
fn note(db: &mut Db, text: &str, about: &[&str], ts: i64) -> String {
    let about: Vec<String> = about.iter().map(|s| (*s).to_string()).collect();
    remember(
        db,
        &RememberInput {
            text,
            about: &about,
            kind: "note",
            ts,
            source: None,
            entities: &[],
            facts: &[],
        },
    )
    .unwrap()
    .note
}

fn same_team_rule() -> RuleDef {
    RuleDef {
        name: "same_team".into(),
        src_label: "Person".into(),
        dst_label: "Person".into(),
        predicate: Predicate::FieldEqual {
            field: "team".into(),
        },
        edge_type: "SAME_TEAM".into(),
        weight_prop: Some("weight".into()),
        max_edges: Some(32),
        approximate: false,
        via_label: None,
        via_edge: None,
        via_dir: None,
        namespace: None,
    }
}

#[test]
fn forgetting_a_node_lists_the_notes_about_it_and_keeps_them() {
    let mut db = store("node");
    // `ada` does not exist, so `remember` stubs it as a provisional Entity
    // and links each note to it with a hand-written ABOUT edge.
    let mut notes: Vec<String> = (0..12)
        .map(|i| note(&mut db, &format!("Ada fact number {i}"), &["ada"], 1_759_000_000 + i))
        .collect();
    notes.sort();

    let report = forget(&mut db, &ForgetTarget::Node { key: "ada".into() }).unwrap();

    assert_eq!(report.mode, "node");
    assert_eq!(report.target, "ada (Entity)");
    assert!(report.changed);
    assert_eq!(report.manual_edges, 12, "one ABOUT edge per note");
    assert_eq!(report.derived_edges, 0, "a memory store ships no rules");
    assert_eq!(report.notes_total, 12);
    assert_eq!(report.notes, notes[..FORGET_NOTE_LIST], "the first ten, sorted");
    assert!(!db.has_node("ada"));
    for key in &notes {
        assert!(db.has_node(key), "a note is listed, never deleted: {key}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn an_unknown_key_is_an_error_and_an_absent_property_is_not() {
    let mut db = store("prop");
    note(&mut db, "Ada likes Rust", &["ada"], 1_759_000_000);

    let missing = forget(&mut db, &ForgetTarget::Node { key: "nobody".into() });
    assert!(
        matches!(missing, Err(GraphError::KeyNotFound { ref key }) if key == "nobody"),
        "{missing:?}"
    );

    let absent = forget(
        &mut db,
        &ForgetTarget::Prop {
            key: "ada".into(),
            prop: "no_such_prop".into(),
        },
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(absent.mode, "prop");
    assert_eq!(absent.target, "ada.no_such_prop");
    assert!(!absent.changed, "nothing was set, so nothing was written");
    assert_eq!(absent.derived_edges, 0);

    // Removing a property that is set is a change. `aliases_rewritten` is
    // carried, not asserted, here: a stub's name is its key, so the list the
    // key alone implies is the list it already has. The server's
    // `forgetting_a_name_takes_its_words_out_of_aliases_and_says_so` pins the
    // case where the name's words do leave.
    let named = forget(
        &mut db,
        &ForgetTarget::Prop {
            key: "ada".into(),
            prop: "name".into(),
        },
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert!(named.changed);
    assert_eq!(named.prop.as_deref(), Some("name"));
    assert_eq!(db.get_prop("ada", "name"), None);
}

#[test]
fn a_rule_derived_fact_is_refused_with_the_rule_and_its_fields_named() {
    let mut db = store("owned");
    for key in ["a", "b"] {
        db.insert_node("Person", key, vec![("team".into(), Value::Str("red".into()))])
            .unwrap();
    }
    db.create_rule(same_team_rule()).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        db.neighbors("a", "SAME_TEAM", Direction::Out).unwrap(),
        vec!["b".to_string()],
        "fixture: the rule derived the edge"
    );

    let refused = forget(
        &mut db,
        &ForgetTarget::Fact {
            subject: "a".into(),
            predicate: "SAME_TEAM".into(),
            object: "b".into(),
        },
    );
    match refused {
        Err(GraphError::RuleOwned { detail }) => assert_eq!(
            detail,
            "refused: SAME_TEAM a → b is derived by rule same_team. It changes only when \
             the fields that rule reads change (team), or when the rule is deleted. \
             Nothing was written."
        ),
        other => panic!("expected a RuleOwned refusal, got {other:?}"),
    }
    assert_eq!(
        db.neighbors("a", "SAME_TEAM", Direction::Out).unwrap(),
        vec!["b".to_string()],
        "nothing was written"
    );
}

#[test]
fn a_hand_written_fact_is_retracted_once_and_the_shapes_are_exclusive() {
    let mut db = store("fact");
    for key in ["a", "b"] {
        db.insert_node("Person", key, vec![]).unwrap();
    }
    db.insert_edge("KNOWS", "a", "b").unwrap();
    let fact = ForgetTarget::Fact {
        subject: "a".into(),
        predicate: "KNOWS".into(),
        object: "b".into(),
    };

    let first = forget(&mut db, &fact).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(first.mode, "fact");
    assert_eq!(first.target, "KNOWS a → b");
    assert!(first.changed);
    assert!(first.notes.is_empty(), "no note is about both ends");

    let second = forget(&mut db, &fact).unwrap();
    assert!(!second.changed, "already gone: nothing to retract");

    // Exactly one of the three shapes.
    let s = |v: &str| Some(v.to_string());
    let triple = Some(("a".to_string(), "KNOWS".to_string(), "b".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(
        ForgetTarget::from_parts(s("a"), None, None),
        Some(ForgetTarget::Node { key: "a".into() })
    );
    assert_eq!(
        ForgetTarget::from_parts(s("a"), s("team"), None),
        Some(ForgetTarget::Prop {
            key: "a".into(),
            prop: "team".into()
        })
    );
    assert_eq!(ForgetTarget::from_parts(None, None, triple.clone()), Some(fact));
    assert_eq!(ForgetTarget::from_parts(None, None, None), None);
    assert_eq!(ForgetTarget::from_parts(None, s("team"), None), None);
    assert_eq!(ForgetTarget::from_parts(s("a"), None, triple), None);
}
```

Add `pub mod forget;` to `crates/core-api/src/memory/mod.rs`, between `pub mod brief;` and `pub mod identity;`. Create `crates/core-api/src/memory/forget.rs`. **The listing below is `ae4d6c6`'s `tool_forget`, after plan 3's second amendment**: the report carries `aliases_rewritten`, and the property arm rewrites `aliases` in the same commit when a `name` is forgotten. If the head has moved again, the head wins, in the struct and in the arm. Everything below the imports is the server's code, moved: the bodies of the three match arms, `notes_about`, `derived_edges_on`, `predicate_fields` and `rule_owned_refusal` are the lines at `crates/server/src/mcp_tasks.rs:2138-2177`, `:2295-2412` and `:2419` on, with `g` renamed `db`, `core_api::` paths made `crate::`, each `return CallOutcome::ToolErr(graph_err_msg(e))` made `?`, and the `RealFs` parameter made generic. **Open the server file beside this one and move the code; do not retype it.**

```rust
//! `forget` — tombstone a node, remove one property, or retract one fact, and
//! say what is left behind.
//!
//! The decision and the report live here so that every surface gives the
//! same answer: the MCP `forget` tool renders [`ForgetReport`] as prose, and
//! the Python binding returns it as a dict. Before 0.7's last plan this was
//! private to the MCP server.
//!
//! A tombstone is not a redaction. History still holds what was forgotten
//! until the log is pruned, and a note that stated a forgotten fact still
//! says it: the report lists those notes and deletes none of them.

use crate::digest::sanitize;
use crate::memory::identity::{
    identity_props_after_forgetting_name, ALIASES_FIELD, ALIAS_KEYS_FIELD,
};
use crate::memory_schema::NAME_FIELD;
use crate::{GraphDb, PredicateSummary, RuleDef};
use core_storage::fs::Fs;
use core_storage::{Direction, GraphError, Result};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

/// Notes a report names when what it forgot was written about; the rest are
/// counted in [`ForgetReport::notes_total`].
pub const FORGET_NOTE_LIST: usize = 10;

/// The refusal for a call that is not exactly one of the three shapes.
pub const FORGET_SHAPE: &str = "pass exactly one of: key (forget a node), key and prop \
     (forget one property), or fact {subject, predicate, object} (retract one edge)";

/// What to forget: exactly one of a node, one property, or one fact.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ForgetTarget {
    /// Tombstone the node and every edge on it.
    Node { key: String },
    /// Remove one property from a node.
    Prop { key: String, prop: String },
    /// Retract one hand-written edge, `(predicate, subject, object)`.
    Fact {
        subject: String,
        predicate: String,
        object: String,
    },
}

impl ForgetTarget {
    /// The target three optional arguments name, or `None` when they are not
    /// exactly one of the three shapes — the caller answers [`FORGET_SHAPE`].
    #[must_use]
    pub fn from_parts(
        key: Option<String>,
        prop: Option<String>,
        fact: Option<(String, String, String)>,
    ) -> Option<Self> {
        match (key, prop, fact) {
            (Some(key), None, None) => Some(Self::Node { key }),
            (Some(key), Some(prop), None) => Some(Self::Prop { key, prop }),
            (None, None, Some((subject, predicate, object))) => Some(Self::Fact {
                subject,
                predicate,
                object,
            }),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
}

/// What one `forget` call did.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct ForgetReport {
    /// `node`, `prop` or `fact`.
    pub mode: &'static str,
    /// The node, property or edge named, as the caller named it.
    pub target: String,
    /// False when there was nothing to forget, and nothing was written.
    pub changed: bool,
    /// Edges removed with a node: written by hand, and derived by rules. For a
    /// property, `derived_edges` counts the rule-derived edges on the node that
    /// the removal retracted, because a rule read that property.
    pub manual_edges: u64,
    pub derived_edges: u64,
    /// The property removed, in `prop` mode.
    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub prop: Option<String>,
    /// Notes with an `ABOUT` edge to the node, or to both ends of the fact.
    /// Listed, never deleted: their text still says what was forgotten.
    pub notes: Vec<String>,
    pub notes_total: usize,
    /// The first commit history still answers from.
    pub history_floor: u64,
    /// `prop` mode removed `name` from a node that carries `aliases`, and
    /// rewrote that list in the same write: the name's words left it.
    pub aliases_rewritten: bool,
    /// `prop` mode removed `aliases` from a node that still carries
    /// `alias_keys`, the aliases it declared, which still link stubs.
    pub alias_keys_remain: bool,
}

/// Every field a predicate reads, its parts' included, sorted and deduped.
#[must_use]
pub fn predicate_fields(p: &PredicateSummary) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut out: BTreeSet<String> = p.fields.iter().cloned().collect();
    for part in p.parts.iter().flatten() {
        out.extend(predicate_fields(part));
    }
    out.into_iter().collect()
}

/// Notes with an `ABOUT` edge to every one of `keys`, sorted.
fn notes_about<F: Fs>(db: &GraphDb<F>, keys: &[&str]) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut common: Option<BTreeSet<String>> = None;
    for key in keys {
        let into: BTreeSet<String> = db
            .neighbors(key, "ABOUT", Direction::In)
            .unwrap_or_default()
            .into_iter()
            .filter(|k| db.node_ref(k).is_some_and(|n| n.label() == "Note"))
            .collect();
        common = Some(match common {
            None => into,
            Some(c) => c.intersection(&into).cloned().collect(),
        });
    }
    common.unwrap_or_default().into_iter().collect()
}

/// An edge as `(edge_type, src_key, dst_key)`.
type EdgeTriple = (String, String, String);

/// The rule-derived edges incident on `key`, both directions, each once.
fn derived_edges_on<F: Fs>(db: &GraphDb<F>, key: &str) -> Result<BTreeSet<EdgeTriple>> {
    Ok(db
        .node_edges(key)?
        .into_iter()
        .filter(|e| e.derived)
        .map(|e| (e.edge_type, e.src_key, e.dst_key))
        .collect())
}

/// Forget `target`, in one call on the one handle, so nothing can change
/// between what the report says and what was done.
///
/// # Errors
/// - [`GraphError::KeyNotFound`] for a node or a fact endpoint that does not
///   exist. Nothing is written.
/// - [`GraphError::RuleOwned`] for a fact a rule derived. `detail` is the
///   whole refusal — which rule owns the edge and the fields it reads
///   ([`rule_owned_refusal`]) — and nothing is written.
/// - Any other engine refusal, as it came.
pub fn forget<F: Fs>(db: &mut GraphDb<F>, target: &ForgetTarget) -> Result<ForgetReport> {
    match target {
        ForgetTarget::Node { key } => {
            if !db.has_node(key) {
                return Err(GraphError::KeyNotFound { key: key.clone() });
            }
            let notes = notes_about(db, &[key.as_str()]);
            let label = db
                .node_ref(key)
                .map(|n| n.label().to_string())
                .unwrap_or_default();
            let deleted = db.delete_node(key)?;
            Ok(ForgetReport {
                mode: "node",
                target: format!("{key} ({label})"),
                changed: true,
                manual_edges: deleted.manual_edges,
                derived_edges: deleted.derived_edges,
                prop: None,
                notes_total: notes.len(),
                notes: notes.into_iter().take(FORGET_NOTE_LIST).collect(),
                history_floor: db.stats().history_floor,
                aliases_rewritten: false,
                alias_keys_remain: false,
            })
        }
        ForgetTarget::Prop { key, prop } => {
            // Removing a property re-runs rule retraction, so a rule that read
            // it drops the edges it derived. Compare the node's derived edges
            // either side of the write to say how many went.
            let before = derived_edges_on(db, key)?;
            // A name's words live in `aliases`. When the name goes, the list is
            // rewritten from the key alone in the same commit, so the words
            // leave with it. A node with no `aliases` list, or one holding a
            // value the store cannot read as a list, is left as it is.
            let rewrite = if prop == NAME_FIELD && db.get_prop(key, prop).is_some() {
                identity_props_after_forgetting_name(db, key).unwrap_or_default()
            } else {
                Vec::new()
            };
            let changed = if rewrite.is_empty() {
                db.remove_prop(key, prop)?
            } else {
                let mut batch = db.batch();
                batch.remove_prop(key, prop);
                for (field, value) in &rewrite {
                    batch.set_prop(key, field, value.clone());
                }
                batch.commit().map(|_| true)?
            };
            let aliases_rewritten = changed && !rewrite.is_empty();
            let alias_keys_remain =
                changed && prop == ALIASES_FIELD && db.get_prop(key, ALIAS_KEYS_FIELD).is_some();
            let retracted = if changed {
                let after = derived_edges_on(db, key).unwrap_or_default();
                before.difference(&after).count() as u64
            } else {
                0
            };
            Ok(ForgetReport {
                mode: "prop",
                target: format!("{key}.{prop}"),
                changed,
                manual_edges: 0,
                derived_edges: retracted,
                prop: Some(prop.clone()),
                notes: Vec::new(),
                notes_total: 0,
                history_floor: db.stats().history_floor,
                aliases_rewritten,
                alias_keys_remain,
            })
        }
        ForgetTarget::Fact {
            subject,
            predicate,
            object,
        } => {
            let changed = match db.delete_edge(predicate, subject, object) {
                Ok(c) => c,
                Err(GraphError::RuleOwned { detail }) => {
                    return Err(GraphError::RuleOwned {
                        detail: rule_owned_refusal(db, predicate, subject, object, &detail),
                    })
                }
                Err(e) => return Err(e),
            };
            let notes = if changed {
                notes_about(db, &[subject.as_str(), object.as_str()])
            } else {
                Vec::new()
            };
            Ok(ForgetReport {
                mode: "fact",
                target: format!("{predicate} {subject} → {object}"),
                changed,
                manual_edges: 0,
                derived_edges: 0,
                prop: None,
                notes_total: notes.len(),
                notes: notes.into_iter().take(FORGET_NOTE_LIST).collect(),
                history_floor: db.stats().history_floor,
                aliases_rewritten: false,
                alias_keys_remain: false,
            })
        }
    }
}

/// Why a fact edge cannot be retracted: which rule owns it, and the only two
/// ways it can change.
pub fn rule_owned_refusal<F: Fs>(
    db: &GraphDb<F>,
    predicate: &str,
    subject: &str,
    object: &str,
    detail: &str,
) -> String {
    let label = |k: &str| db.node_ref(k).map(|n| n.label().to_string());
    let (src, dst) = (label(subject), label(object));
    let owners: Vec<RuleDef> = db
        .rules()
        .into_iter()
        .filter(|r| {
            r.edge_type == predicate
                && Some(&r.src_label) == src.as_ref()
                && Some(&r.dst_label) == dst.as_ref()
        })
        .collect();
    if owners.is_empty() {
        return sanitize(detail);
    }
    let names: Vec<String> = owners.iter().map(|r| sanitize(&r.name)).collect();
    let mut fields: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
    for r in &owners {
        fields.extend(predicate_fields(&PredicateSummary::from(&r.predicate)));
    }
    let fields: Vec<String> = fields.iter().map(|f| sanitize(f)).collect();
    format!(
        "refused: {} {} → {} is derived by rule {}. It changes only when the fields \
         that rule reads change ({}), or when the rule is deleted. Nothing was written.",
        sanitize(predicate),
        sanitize(subject),
        sanitize(object),
        names.join(", "),
        fields.join(", ")
    )
}
```

Two things to check by eye before moving on. The server's property arm compared `prop == core_api::memory_schema::NAME_FIELD` with `prop: String`; here `prop` is a `&String` against a `&str` constant — if the compiler objects, write `prop.as_str() == NAME_FIELD`. And `identity_props_after_forgetting_name<F: Fs>(db: &GraphDb<F>, key: &str) -> Result<Vec<(String, Value)>>` is read through `&mut GraphDb<F>` reborrowed immutably, finished before `db.batch()` borrows it mutably — the order above, which is the server's.

In `crates/server/src/mcp_tasks.rs`: delete the seven items listed under **Files**. Add to the imports at the top:

```rust
use core_api::memory::forget::{
    forget, predicate_fields, ForgetReport, ForgetTarget, FORGET_SHAPE,
};
```

(`predicate_fields` is still used by `tool_suggest_rules` until Task 4.) Replace the whole of `tool_forget` with:

```rust
fn tool_forget(db: &SharedDb, args: &Js, json_out: bool) -> CallOutcome {
    let key = match opt_str_arg(args, "key") {
        Ok(k) => k.map(str::to_string),
        Err(e) => return CallOutcome::ToolErr(e),
    };
    let prop = match opt_str_arg(args, "prop") {
        Ok(p) => p.map(str::to_string),
        Err(e) => return CallOutcome::ToolErr(e),
    };
    let fact = match args.get("fact") {
        None | Some(Js::Null) => None,
        Some(v) if v.is_object() => match fact_arg(v) {
            Ok(f) => Some(f),
            Err(e) => return CallOutcome::ToolErr(e),
        },
        Some(_) => return CallOutcome::ToolErr("fact must be an object".into()),
    };
    let Some(target) = ForgetTarget::from_parts(key, prop, fact) else {
        return CallOutcome::ToolErr(FORGET_SHAPE.into());
    };
    // Only a fact's refusal is the enriched sentence. Any other `RuleOwned`
    // is rendered the way every engine error is.
    let is_fact = matches!(target, ForgetTarget::Fact { .. });
    // One write guard for the reads the report needs and the write itself, so
    // nothing can change between what the reply says and what was done.
    let report = {
        let mut g = db.write();
        match forget(&mut *g, &target) {
            Ok(r) => r,
            Err(GraphError::RuleOwned { detail }) if is_fact => {
                return CallOutcome::ToolErr(detail)
            }
            Err(e) => return CallOutcome::ToolErr(graph_err_msg(e)),
        }
    };
    ok(json_out, &report, render_forget)
}
```

`render_forget`, `history_line`, `ALIASES_REWRITTEN_LINE` and `alias_keys_remain_line` are unchanged: they read `ForgetReport` by field name, and the names did not change. `rule_owned_refusal` is no longer defined here; nothing else in the file called it.

### Task 4: The suggestion filter and the entity upsert live in `core-api` (relocation, R1)

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/core-api/src/memory/suggest.rs`
- Create: `crates/core-api/tests/memory_suggest.rs`, `crates/core-api/tests/memory_upsert.rs`
- Modify: `crates/core-api/src/memory/mod.rs` (`pub mod suggest;`, after `schema`)
- Modify: `crates/core-api/src/memory/remember.rs` — `UpsertOutcome`, `upsert_entity`, `DEFAULT_NOTE_KIND`, `unix_now_secs`, `dedup_keep_order`
- Modify: `crates/server/src/mcp_tasks.rs` — delete `BOOKKEEPING_FIELDS` (`:2477`), `SuggestionOut` (`:2490`), `create_rule_args` (`:2554`), `round_to` (`:2722`); rewrite `tool_suggest_rules` (`:2641`); three lines of `tool_remember` (`:1862`)
- Modify: `crates/server/src/mcp.rs` — `tool_upsert_entity` (`:657-800`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `core_api::memory::forget::predicate_fields` (Task 3).
- Produces, in `core_api::memory::suggest`:
  - `pub const BOOKKEEPING_FIELDS: [&str; 8]`
  - `pub fn round_to(x: f64, places: i32) -> f64`
  - `pub fn create_rule_args(def: &RuleDef) -> serde_json::Value`
  - `pub struct Suggestion { pub name, pub src_label, pub dst_label, pub edge_type, pub predicate: String, pub est_edges: u64, pub examples: Vec<(String, String, f64)>, pub rationale: String, pub create_rule_args: serde_json::Value }` — `Serialize`
  - `pub struct FilteredSuggestions { pub suggestions: Vec<Suggestion>, pub total: usize, pub bookkeeping_hidden: usize, pub truncated: bool }` — `Serialize`
  - `pub fn filtered_suggestions<F: Fs>(db: &GraphDb<F>) -> FilteredSuggestions` — every proposal that survives the filter, **uncapped**
- Produces, in `core_api::memory::remember`:
  - `pub const DEFAULT_NOTE_KIND: &str = "note";`
  - `pub fn unix_now_secs() -> i64`
  - `pub fn dedup_keep_order(keys: &mut Vec<String>)`
  - `pub struct UpsertOutcome { pub label: String, pub created: bool, pub updated_fields: usize, pub same_as_lost: Vec<SameAsPair> }`
  - `pub fn upsert_entity<F: Fs>(db: &mut GraphDb<F>, key: &str, label: Option<&str>, row: BTreeMap<String, Value>, aliases: &[String]) -> Result<UpsertOutcome>`
  Tasks 6 and 7 call all of them from the binding.

**Why:** Controller R1, and correction S8. Three pieces of server policy stand between the binding and parity:

1. **The bookkeeping filter.** Plan 3 measured that on a store `remember` filled, *every* raw engine proposal is a clique rule over `kind`, `source` or `ts`. A Python `suggest_rules` wrapping `GraphDb::suggest_rules` directly would return exactly the proposals MCP hides.
2. **`create_rule_args`.** It writes `weight_prop` explicitly and drops nulls. A raw serialised `RuleDef` carries `"weight_prop": null`, which MCP's `create_rule` defaults to `"weight"` and Python's leaves `None` — two different rules from one suggestion.
3. **The upsert policy.** `describe_entity_with_aliases` maintains aliases and clears `provisional`, but the relabel refusal, the "label required" refusal, the rule that `id` never comes from `props`, the namespace no-op and the `same_as_lost` report are in `tool_upsert_entity`.

`MAX_SUGGESTIONS` (five to a reply) and the identity-preset offer stay in the server: the first is how much one message can carry, and the second names a store path only the server knows. `render_suggestions` stays too.

**Byte identity.** The server struct `SuggestOut` keeps its five fields in their order; its `suggestions` become `Vec<core_api::memory::suggest::Suggestion>`, whose nine fields are `SuggestionOut`'s nine in the same order. `tool_upsert_entity`'s two refusals were bare strings and are now `IngestError { detail }`, which `graph_err_msg` (`mcp.rs:1205`) renders as the bare `detail` — the same bytes. The pins, unedited: `crates/server/tests/mcp.rs:4544`, `:4731`, `:4784`, `:4880`, `:4928`, `:5632`, `:5969`, `:6403` (`upsert_entity_reports_the_full_name_stub_a_rename_unlinks`), `:6579`, `:6609`, `:6631`, and the unit test at `crates/server/src/mcp_tasks.rs` `an_empty_truncated_suggestion_run_does_not_claim_full_coverage`, which builds a `SuggestOut` literal and must compile unchanged.
