### Task 10: Measure the prompt hook on a large store (R4)

**Files:**
- Create: `benchmarks/hook-latency/PROCEDURE.md` — written and committed **before** the run
- Create: `crates/cli/examples/hook_latency_store.rs` — builds the store
- Create: `scripts/measure-hook-latency.py` — times the hook
- Create: `benchmarks/hook-latency/results/<UTC timestamp>.md` — the run, as it came out
- Modify: `docs/roadmap/v0.6.10-defects.md` (row 38)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: a recorded number. **No gate, no threshold that fails anything** (R4). Task 15's changelog may quote it; nothing else depends on it.

**Why:** Controller R4; ledger row 38. The `UserPromptSubmit` hook runs `mushroomdb recall --auto` on **every** prompt with a 5-second timeout (`scripts/plugin-templates/hooks.json.tmpl:8-9`). The only measurement on record is about 0.24 s on a store ingested from this repository — roughly ten thousand nodes. Two things have changed since: plan 2 made the hook answer every prompt rather than only those naming an identifier, and every store now declares full-text, whose index is rebuilt from scratch on each open (ledger row 36). The hook is a fresh process per prompt, so it pays that open every time. Whether a 100,000-note store answers inside 5 seconds is not known, and a hook that times out is silent — the user sees nothing and concludes memory does not work.

**This is a measurement, so the procedure is committed before the number exists**, the way plan 3's pre-registrations were. It has no floor to pass: what is pre-registered is what is built, what is timed, and what is reported, so the number cannot be chosen after the fact.

**It needs a release build.** A debug build's timings are not publishable, and this is the one task in the plan that makes one. It is the binary only (`cargo build --release -p mushroomdb-cli --bin mushroomdb`, no `embed-ui`), behind Procedure P1's disk check.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the procedure and commit it alone**

Create `benchmarks/hook-latency/PROCEDURE.md`:

```markdown
# Prompt-hook latency on a large memory store — procedure

Written 2026-10-01, before the first run. No result existed when this was committed.

## The question

How long does the `UserPromptSubmit` hook take on a memory store of 100,000 notes, against its
5-second timeout? The hook is `mushroomdb recall <db>` with the prompt on stdin, one fresh
process per prompt. Its cost is opening the store plus one `recall`.

This is a measurement, not a gate. Nothing fails on its result.

## The store

Built by `cargo run --release -p mushroomdb-cli --example hook_latency_store -- <dir> 100000`:

- the default memory schema (`memory_defaults()`);
- 1,000 `Person` nodes, `person-0000` … `person-0999`, each with a two-word `name`;
- 100,000 `Note` nodes, each with a `text` of twelve words drawn from a fixed 2,000-word
  vocabulary by a seeded generator (seed 7), a `kind`, a `ts`, and one `ABOUT` edge to a person;
- one `snapshot()` at the end, so the hook opens from a snapshot as a long-lived store does.

The generator is deterministic: the same command builds the same store.

## What is timed

`scripts/measure-hook-latency.py --binary target/release/mushroomdb --db <dir> --runs 20`

For each of three prompts, 20 runs, each a new process, wall clock from spawn to exit:

1. a name the store holds — `"wlomipuka wlomipulo"`, the two words of `person-0421`'s name;
2. two common words of the vocabulary — `"wkakakaka wlokakaka"`, each in about 600 notes;
3. a prompt that matches nothing — `"zzqx vvkw"`.

And, as the baseline that separates open cost from recall cost, 20 runs of
`mushroomdb stats <db>`, which opens the store and does almost nothing else.

File cache warm (one untimed run of each first). Release build. No other load the operator
knows of; the machine and its load average are recorded in the result.

## What is reported

For each of the four: median, p95 and max in milliseconds, and for the three prompts the size
of the digest in bytes. The result file also records the commit, the binary's version, the
machine, the store's size on disk, and whether any run exceeded 5,000 ms.

Everything is reported, whatever it shows. If a run exceeds the timeout that is the finding,
and it goes in the ledger (row 38) and the changelog's known limits, not into a retry.

## What this does not measure

A cold file cache; a store with the identity preset applied; a store on a network volume; the
`SessionStart` brief, which has its own 3-second internal budget.
```

```bash
git add benchmarks/hook-latency/PROCEDURE.md
git commit -m "test(bench): pre-register the prompt-hook latency measurement"
```

- [ ] **Step 2: The store builder**

Create `crates/cli/examples/hook_latency_store.rs`:

```rust
//! Build the store `benchmarks/hook-latency/PROCEDURE.md` describes.
//!
//!   cargo run --release -p mushroomdb-cli --example hook_latency_store -- <dir> <notes>
//!
//! Deterministic: a seeded generator, so the same arguments build the same
//! store. Refuses a directory that already exists.
use core_api::memory_schema::memory_defaults;
use core_api::{GraphDb, Value};

const PEOPLE: usize = 1_000;
const VOCABULARY: usize = 2_000;
const WORDS_PER_NOTE: usize = 12;
const CHUNK: usize = 2_000;
const SEED: u64 = 7;

/// The generator `core_rules::suggest` uses for sampling, restated: a 64-bit
/// LCG. Good enough to spread words; not for anything else.
fn next(state: &mut u64) -> u64 {
    *state = state
        .wrapping_mul(6_364_136_223_846_793_005)
        .wrapping_add(1_442_695_040_888_963_407);
    *state >> 33
}

/// Word `i` of the vocabulary: pronounceable, distinct, and never a stopword.
fn word(i: usize) -> String {
    const SYLLABLES: [&str; 10] = ["ka", "lo", "mi", "ne", "pu", "ra", "so", "ti", "vu", "ze"];
    let mut out = String::from("w");
    let mut n = i;
    for _ in 0..4 {
        out.push_str(SYLLABLES[n % 10]);
        n /= 10;
    }
    out
}

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().collect();
    if args.len() != 3 {
        eprintln!("usage: hook_latency_store <dir> <notes>");
        std::process::exit(2);
    }
    let dir = std::path::PathBuf::from(&args[1]);
    let notes: usize = args[2].parse().expect("<notes> must be a number");
    if dir.exists() {
        eprintln!("{} already exists; pass a fresh path", dir.display());
        std::process::exit(2);
    }

    let mut db = GraphDb::open(&dir).expect("open");
    db.apply_schema(&memory_defaults()).expect("schema");

    let mut batch = db.batch();
    for p in 0..PEOPLE {
        batch.insert_node(
            "Person",
            &format!("person-{p:04}"),
            vec![(
                "name".into(),
                Value::Str(format!("{} {}", word(p), word(p + PEOPLE))),
            )],
        );
    }
    batch.commit().expect("people");

    let mut state = SEED;
    let mut written = 0usize;
    while written < notes {
        let upto = (written + CHUNK).min(notes);
        let mut batch = db.batch();
        for n in written..upto {
            let text: Vec<String> = (0..WORDS_PER_NOTE)
                .map(|_| word((next(&mut state) as usize) % VOCABULARY))
                .collect();
            let key = format!("note:{n:08}");
            batch.insert_node(
                "Note",
                &key,
                vec![
                    ("text".into(), Value::Str(text.join(" "))),
                    ("kind".into(), Value::Str("note".into())),
                    ("ts".into(), Value::Int(1_759_000_000 + n as i64)),
                ],
            );
            let person = format!("person-{:04}", (next(&mut state) as usize) % PEOPLE);
            batch.insert_edge("ABOUT", &key, &person);
        }
        batch.commit().expect("notes");
        written = upto;
    }
    db.snapshot().expect("snapshot");
    println!(
        "built {} people and {notes} notes at {}",
        PEOPLE,
        dir.display()
    );
}
```

Read before building: `BatchBuilder::insert_node(&mut self, label: &str, key: &str, props: Vec<(String, Value)>) -> &mut Self` (`db.rs:15007`) and `BatchBuilder::insert_edge` — the binding calls it with `(edge_type, src, dst)` (`bindings/python/src/lib.rs:1505`); an insert and an edge to it in one batch is the shape `remember` commits. `GraphDb::snapshot(&mut self) -> Result<()>` is `db.rs:13572`. The `cli` crate already depends on `core-api` (`crates/cli/Cargo.toml:36`), so the example needs no manifest change — Cargo discovers `examples/*.rs`.

- [ ] **Step 3: The timer**

Create `scripts/measure-hook-latency.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Time the prompt hook against a store: `mushroomdb recall <db>`, one process per prompt.

    python3 scripts/measure-hook-latency.py --binary target/release/mushroomdb \
        --db /path/to/store [--runs 20]

Prints a Markdown report on stdout. Measures; fails nothing. Exits 2 only when
it cannot run at all (no binary, no store). The procedure it implements is
benchmarks/hook-latency/PROCEDURE.md.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import platform
import statistics
import subprocess
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path

TIMEOUT_MS = 5_000  # the hook's own timeout, scripts/plugin-templates/hooks.json.tmpl

PROMPTS = [
    # person-0421's name, as the builder writes it: word(421) and word(1421).
    ("a name the store holds", "wlomipuka wlomipulo"),
    # word(0) and word(1): each occurs in about 600 of 100,000 notes.
    ("two common words", "wkakakaka wlokakaka"),
    ("no match", "zzqx vvkw"),
]


def run_once(cmd: list[str], stdin: str) -> tuple[float, bytes]:
    start = time.perf_counter()
    out = subprocess.run(cmd, input=stdin.encode(), capture_output=True, check=False)
    return (time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000.0, out.stdout


def series(cmd: list[str], stdin: str, runs: int) -> tuple[list[float], int]:
    run_once(cmd, stdin)  # warm the file cache; not timed
    times, size = [], 0
    for _ in range(runs):
        ms, out = run_once(cmd, stdin)
        times.append(ms)
        size = len(out)
    return times, size


def row(label: str, times: list[float], size: int | None) -> str:
    ordered = sorted(times)
    p95 = ordered[max(0, int(round(0.95 * len(ordered))) - 1)]
    cells = [
        label,
        f"{statistics.median(ordered):.0f}",
        f"{p95:.0f}",
        f"{ordered[-1]:.0f}",
        "—" if size is None else str(size),
    ]
    return "| " + " | ".join(cells) + " |"


def dir_bytes(path: Path) -> int:
    return sum(f.stat().st_size for f in path.rglob("*") if f.is_file())


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    ap.add_argument("--binary", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--db", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--runs", type=int, default=20)
    a = ap.parse_args()

    binary, db = Path(a.binary), Path(a.db)
    if not binary.is_file():
        print(f"measure-hook-latency: no binary at {binary}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
    if not db.is_dir():
        print(f"measure-hook-latency: no store at {db}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2

    version = subprocess.run(
        [str(binary), "--version"], capture_output=True, text=True, check=False
    ).stdout.strip()
    commit = subprocess.run(
        ["git", "rev-parse", "--short", "HEAD"], capture_output=True, text=True, check=False
    ).stdout.strip()

    lines = [
        "# Prompt-hook latency",
        "",
        f"- **Commit:** `{commit}`",
        f"- **Binary:** `{binary}` — {version}",
        f"- **Machine:** {platform.machine()}, {platform.system()} {platform.release()}, "
        f"{os.cpu_count()} cores; load average {os.getloadavg()[0]:.2f}",
        f"- **Store:** `{db}` — {dir_bytes(db) / 1_048_576:.1f} MiB on disk",
        f"- **Runs:** {a.runs} per row, one process each, file cache warm",
        f"- **Hook timeout:** {TIMEOUT_MS} ms",
        "",
        "| What | median ms | p95 ms | max ms | digest bytes |",
        "|---|---|---|---|---|",
    ]
    worst = 0.0
    base, _ = series([str(binary), "stats", str(db)], "", a.runs)
    lines.append(row("`stats` — open the store, nothing else", base, None))
    worst = max(worst, max(base))
    for label, prompt in PROMPTS:
        times, size = series(
            [str(binary), "recall", str(db)], json.dumps({"prompt": prompt}), a.runs
        )
        lines.append(row(f"`recall` — {label}: {prompt!r}", times, size))
        worst = max(worst, max(times))
    lines += [
        "",
        f"Slowest single run: {worst:.0f} ms — "
        + ("**over the hook's timeout**" if worst > TIMEOUT_MS else "inside the hook's timeout")
        + ".",
    ]
    print("\n".join(lines))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

The hook's stdin is a JSON object whose `prompt` field carries the text (`prompt_from_payload`, `crates/cli/src/recall.rs:25-36`), and `mushroomdb recall <db-dir>` is the hook body (`crates/cli/src/lib.rs:828-829`).

### Task 11: The tree names no other system, and a gate keeps it so (PD-2)

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/check-names.py`, `scripts/blocked-names.sha256`, `scripts/tests/test-check-names.sh`
- Modify: `scripts/check-claims.sh` (one call, after the `check-pyi.sh` call at `:144-146`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (one step, after `:391`)
- Delete: the three files in `benchmarks/adapters/` other than `__init__.py`, `handrolled.py` and `ours.py`
- Modify: `benchmarks/run.py`, `benchmarks/test_harness.py`, `benchmarks/README.md`
- Create: `benchmarks/results/mushroomdb-10k-0.7.md` (the run)
- Modify: `README.md` (`:88`, `## Benchmarks` at `:361-431`), `llms.txt` (`:512`, `:517`), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (`:254-260`), `docs/site/testing.md` (`:311-313`), `integrations/langchain-mushroomdb/langchain_mushroomdb/graph_store.py` (`:111`, `:190`)
- Modify, names replaced by placeholders: every tracked file under `docs/roadmap/`, `benchmarks/results/`, `docs/release-notes/`, `benchmarks/agent-tasks/results/`, and `docs/design.md`, that the gate reports
- Modify: whatever else the gate reports — code comments under `crates/`, `dogfood/`, `docs/site/`
- Regenerate: `llms-full.txt`
- **Local only, never committed:** `.superpowers/blocked-names.txt`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: `python3 scripts/check-names.py` — exit 0 when no tracked file holds a blocked name, exit 1 printing `path:line` for each (never the name — a path component that is a name prints as `[blocked]`), `--add` to extend the list from stdin. Run by `scripts/check-claims.sh`, so by CI's `plugin-validate` job and by every later task's "check that no claim crept in" step. Task 13 builds on the `## Benchmarks` section this task leaves.

**Why:** PD-2. The owner's standing rule, stated 2026-09-03, is that nothing committed names or compares against another project. The tree breaks it in about two dozen files: the README's front-page table heading, its honesty notes, three adapter **file names**, the benchmark runner, released notes, old plans, and comments. Nothing mechanical has ever enforced the rule, which is why.

**This plan does not name them, and neither may any step's output that gets committed.** The executor needs the list to do the work; it comes from places that are already local or already tracked, and it goes into one gitignored file.

**What the gate can and cannot see (RF-5, and two honest limits).**

- It hashes every word of every tracked file — letters and digits, lowercased, accents folded — **and every prefix and suffix of each word of four characters or more**, and it hashes each path the same way. So a name glued to a suffix in an identifier, a driver package or a file name is caught: that is how three adapter files went unnoticed, and a whole-word check would pass them.
- It does **not** see a name written as two words, or split by a hyphen. Joining adjacent words would make one name on the owner's list match an ordinary two-word phrase this repository uses on nearly every page. Step 5's one-time grep, against the plain local list, is what finds those.
- It cannot hold a name that is also an ordinary word or a standard-library module: the list would then fail on honest prose and on `import` lines. Those few are scrubbed by hand in the places they mean the product, and recorded in a comment in the list file — as a count, not as names.
- It is not secrecy. A dictionary recovers the list from its digests. What it buys is that no tracked file spells a name, the gate needs no exemption for its own list, and CI can run it. PD-2a.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the gate's own test first**

Create `scripts/tests/test-check-names.sh`. It uses invented names only — a fixture must not hold a real one.

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# test-check-names.sh — the names gate, shown passing and failing.
#
# A gate must be seen to fail on what it guards. Every case here is a way a
# name got into this tree, or a way the gate could go wrong:
#
#   - in prose, in any letter case;
#   - glued to a suffix inside an identifier, and as a FILE NAME — the three
#     benchmark adapters were exactly this, and a whole-word check passes them;
#   - spelled with a diacritic;
#   - and the output must never print the name it found.
#
# Invented names only. No real system's name belongs in a fixture.
set -euo pipefail
ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"
GATE="$ROOT/scripts/check-names.py"
TMP="$(mktemp -d)"
trap 'rm -rf "$TMP"' EXIT

LIST="$TMP/list.sha256"
: > "$LIST"
printf 'ExampleGraph\tsystem A\nOtherStore\tsystem B\n' | python3 "$GATE" --list "$LIST" --add >/dev/null
[[ "$(grep -c -E '^[0-9a-f]{64}$' "$LIST")" -eq 2 ]] || { echo "FAIL: --add did not write two digests"; exit 1; }
if grep -qi -E 'examplegraph|otherstore' "$LIST"; then echo "FAIL: the list file spells a name"; exit 1; fi

fails=0
expect() { # description, expected exit, fixture dir
  local out status=0
  out="$(python3 "$GATE" --list "$LIST" --root "$3" 2>&1)" || status=$?
  if [[ "$status" -ne "$2" ]]; then
    echo "FAIL: $1 — exit $status, wanted $2"; echo "$out" | sed 's/^/    /'; fails=1; return
  fi
  if echo "$out" | grep -qi -E 'examplegraph|otherstore'; then
    echo "FAIL: $1 — the output printed the name it found"; fails=1; return
  fi
  echo "ok: $1"
}

d="$TMP/clean"; mkdir -p "$d"; echo "A graph example, and another store of facts." > "$d/a.md"
expect "ordinary words that merely contain parts of a name pass" 0 "$d"

d="$TMP/prose"; mkdir -p "$d"; echo "It is faster than EXAMPLEGRAPH on this workload." > "$d/a.md"
expect "a name in prose, in capitals, fails" 1 "$d"

d="$TMP/glued"; mkdir -p "$d"; echo "class ExampleGraphAdapter: pass" > "$d/a.py"
expect "a name glued to a suffix in an identifier fails" 1 "$d"

d="$TMP/path"; mkdir -p "$d/adapters"; echo "pass" > "$d/adapters/otherstore.py"
expect "a name that is only a file name fails" 1 "$d"

d="$TMP/accent"; mkdir -p "$d"; printf 'Compared with \xc3\x88x\xc3\xa0mpleGraph.\n' > "$d/a.md"

else

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the gate**

Create `scripts/check-names.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""check-names.py — no tracked file names a system on the blocked list.

The standing rule: nothing committed to this repository names or compares
against another project. mushroomdb is described on its own terms.

The list is scripts/blocked-names.sha256: one SHA-256 hex digest per line, of
a name lowercased with everything but letters and digits removed. The names
themselves are in no tracked file, this one included, so the gate needs no
exemption for its own list.

What is checked, for every tracked file and for its path:

  each word (letters and digits, lowercased, accents folded), and every
  prefix and suffix of it at least MIN_LEN characters long.

So a name glued to a suffix — an identifier, a package, a file name — is
found. A name written as two words, or split by a hyphen, is not: joining
adjacent words would make an ordinary phrase match. A name that is also an
ordinary word cannot be listed at all.

    python3 scripts/check-names.py                 check the repository
    python3 scripts/check-names.py --add < names   add names (first tab-separated
                                                   field of each line) to the list

Prints `path:line` for every hit and never the name: a path component that is
itself a name is printed as `[blocked]`. Exits 1 on any hit.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import hashlib
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import unicodedata
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DEFAULT_LIST = ROOT / "scripts" / "blocked-names.sha256"

WORD = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+")
MIN_LEN = 4

# Not text, or not ours: images and fonts, and lockfiles, whose package names
# nobody in this repository chose.
# Content that is not text. The path of such a file is still checked.
BINARY_SUFFIXES = (
    ".png", ".gif", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".ico", ".svg", ".woff", ".woff2", ".pdf",
    ".bin", ".zip", ".gz",
)
EXEMPT_NAMES = ("Cargo.lock", "package-lock.json")


def fold(text: str) -> str:
    """Lowercase, with accents removed: a diacritic must not hide a name."""
    decomposed = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text)
    return "".join(c for c in decomposed if not unicodedata.combining(c)).lower()


def digest(piece: str) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(piece.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


def pieces(word: str):
    """The word, and each prefix and suffix of it at least MIN_LEN long."""
    yield word
    for n in range(MIN_LEN, len(word)):
        yield word[:n]
        yield word[-n:]


def load(path: Path) -> set[str]:
    if not path.is_file():
        return set()
    return {
        line.strip()
        for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
        if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#")
    }


class Matcher:
    def __init__(self, blocked: set[str]) -> None:
        self.blocked = blocked
        self.seen: dict[str, bool] = {}

    def hits(self, text: str) -> bool:
        for word in set(WORD.findall(fold(text))):
            if len(word) < MIN_LEN:
                continue
            known = self.seen.get(word)
            if known is None:
                known = any(digest(p) in self.blocked for p in pieces(word))
                self.seen[word] = known
            if known:
                return True
        return False


def tracked(root: Path) -> list[str]:
    """Tracked files of a checkout; every file under `root` otherwise."""
    probe = subprocess.run(
        ["git", "-C", str(root), "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
        capture_output=True, text=True, check=False,
    )
    if probe.returncode == 0 and Path(probe.stdout.strip()).resolve() == root.resolve():
        out = subprocess.check_output(["git", "-C", str(root), "ls-files", "-z"])
        return [p for p in out.decode("utf-8").split("\0") if p]
    return sorted(str(p.relative_to(root)) for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file())


def add(list_path: Path) -> int:
    have = load(list_path)
    added = 0
    for raw in sys.stdin.read().splitlines():
        name = raw.split("\t", 1)[0].strip()
        if not name or name.startswith("#"):
            continue
        normal = "".join(WORD.findall(fold(name)))
        if len(normal) < MIN_LEN:
            print(
                f"check-names.py: a name of {len(normal)} characters is too short to match "
                f"safely (minimum {MIN_LEN}); nothing was added",
                file=sys.stderr,
            )
            return 2
        d = digest(normal)
        if d not in have:
            have.add(d)
            added += 1
    header = [
        "# Blocked names, as SHA-256 of each name lowercased with only letters and digits kept.",
        "# Add one with: python3 scripts/check-names.py --add   (a name per line on stdin)",
        "# The names themselves are in no tracked file.",
    ]
    kept_comments = [
        line
        for line in (list_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if list_path.is_file() else [])
        if line.startswith("#") and line not in header
    ]
    list_path.write_text("\n".join(header + kept_comments + sorted(have)) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"check-names.py: {added} added, {len(have)} on the list")
    return 0


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="No tracked file names a blocked system.")
    ap.add_argument("--list", type=Path, default=DEFAULT_LIST)
    ap.add_argument("--root", type=Path, default=ROOT)
    ap.add_argument("--add", action="store_true")
    a = ap.parse_args()

    if a.add:
        return add(a.list)

    blocked = load(a.list)
    if not blocked:
        print(f"check-names.py: no digests in {a.list}; nothing to check", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1
    matcher = Matcher(blocked)
    bad = 0
    for rel in tracked(a.root):
        if Path(rel).name in EXEMPT_NAMES:
            continue
        # A path that is itself a name must not be printed: the path would be
        # the name. Each component that hits is shown as `[blocked]`, here and
        # in every `path:line` below.
        shown = "/".join(
            "[blocked]" if matcher.hits(part) else part for part in rel.split("/")
        )
        if shown != rel:
            print(f"{shown}: the path itself")
            bad += 1
        if rel.endswith(BINARY_SUFFIXES):
            continue
        try:
            text = (a.root / rel).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        except (UnicodeDecodeError, OSError):
            continue
        for lineno, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), 1):
            if matcher.hits(line):
                print(f"{shown}:{lineno}")
                bad += 1
    if bad:
        print(
            f"check-names.py: FAILED — {bad} place(s) name a system on the blocked list. "
            "Describe mushroomdb on its own terms; in a historical record, use a neutral "
            "placeholder.",
            file=sys.stderr,
        )
        return 1
    print("check-names.py: OK")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

```bash
set -o pipefail; cd /Users/nilrehsttam/Repos/graph-db && python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib, re, subprocess
pairs = []
for line in pathlib.Path(".superpowers/blocked-names.txt").read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
    if line.strip() and not line.startswith("#"):
        name, _, placeholder = line.partition("\t")
        pairs.append((name.strip(), placeholder.strip() or "a named system"))
pairs.sort(key=lambda p: -len(p[0]))  # longest first, so a name inside a name is not half-replaced
HISTORY = ["docs/roadmap", "benchmarks/results", "docs/release-notes",
           "benchmarks/agent-tasks/results", "docs/design.md", "dogfood/results"]
files = subprocess.check_output(["git", "ls-files", "-z", "--", *HISTORY]).decode().split("\0")
for rel in filter(None, files):
    path = pathlib.Path(rel)
    try:
        before = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    except UnicodeDecodeError:
        continue
    after = before
    for name, placeholder in pairs:
        after = re.sub(r"(?i)\b" + re.escape(name) + r"(?:db)?\b", placeholder, after)
    if after != before:
        path.write_text(after, encoding="utf-8")
        print("rewrote", rel)
EOF

`benchmarks/README.md`: replace the file with:

````markdown
# Benchmark harness

Measures mushroomdb's embedded engine on six workloads at a configurable scale. The engine runs
in the benchmark's own process: no figure here includes a network round-trip or serialization.

## Run it

```bash
# From the repository root, with the binding's virtualenv.
(cd bindings/python && .venv/bin/maturin develop --release)   # a debug build is not worth quoting
bindings/python/.venv/bin/python benchmarks/run.py                    # 10,000 nodes
bindings/python/.venv/bin/python benchmarks/run.py --scale 2000       # the size CI's tests use
bindings/python/.venv/bin/python benchmarks/run.py --scale 10000 --out benchmarks/results/my-run.md
```

Output goes to `benchmarks/results/run-<scale>-<timestamp>.md` unless `--out` names a file. The
run the README quotes is `benchmarks/results/mushroomdb-10k-0.7.md`.

```bash
# The harness's own tests: every workload end to end at 2,000 nodes.
bindings/python/.venv/bin/python -m pytest benchmarks/test_harness.py -v
```

## The workloads

The graph is synthetic and seeded (`datasets.py`): Talent, Company and Job nodes in a 70/20/10
split, with the properties the rules read.

| Workload | What is timed |
|---|---|
| `bulk_ingest` | Inserting every node through `ingest_batch`, 2,000 nodes per call |
| `neighborhood_depth1` | `node_edges` for a sample of keys; p50 and p95 |
| `neighborhood_depth2` | `node_edges`, then each neighbour's; p50 and p95 |
| `cypher_scan_filter` | One `MATCH … WHERE … RETURN` over a label |
| `cypher_two_hop` | A two-hop join through a rule-derived edge type, `LIMIT 200` |
| `rule_derive` | Declaring the rules over the loaded graph: the backfill, per rule and in total |

`cold_start_to_first_query` (opening a store and answering one query) is exercised by the
tests and is not in the results table; the open-time figures the README quotes come from
`dogfood/results/scale-100k.md`.

## What a number here means

- One run, one machine, one process. The results file records the machine, the date and the
  scale; a figure without those is not a figure.
- Latencies under a microsecond are in-memory adjacency reads. They say the engine adds little
  to a pointer walk, not that a service built on it answers in a microsecond.
- `rule_derive` is a one-time cost paid when a rule is declared. Afterwards each write
  re-derives only what it changed.

## Hand-rolled maintenance versus rules

`run_handrolled.py` and `adapters/handrolled.py` compare the rule engine with edge maintenance
written by hand against the same store — per operation, and batched. It is a comparison of two
ways to use mushroomdb, and its result is `benchmarks/results/handrolled-vs-rules.md`.

## Older results

`benchmarks/results/` keeps earlier runs as they were committed. Where an older file compared
engines, the others appear as `system A`, `system B` and `system C`.
````

- [ ] **Step 1: The quick start, as a test**

Append to `crates/cli/tests/first_run.rs`:

```rust
/// The README's quick start, run as printed: remember → recall →
/// suggest_rules → (create_rule, on approval) → explain_association.
///
/// The README may only print a flow this test proves. A dozen people in two
/// teams is the smallest store the flow is claimed for: `suggest_rules` needs
/// enough nodes sharing a value to propose a rule over it.
#[test]
fn the_readme_quick_start_runs_as_printed() {
    let dir = tmp("quickstart");

    // remember ×12 — each a sentence and the entity it names, with a team.
    let mut calls: Vec<Js> = (0..12u64)
        .map(|i| {
            let team = ["infra", "ui"][(i % 2) as usize];
            serde_json::json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":i + 1,"method":"tools/call","params":{
                "name":"remember","arguments":{
                    "text": format!("Person {i} joined the {team} team"),
                    "ts": 1_759_000_000u64 + i,
                    "entities":[{"key": format!("p{i}"), "label":"Person",
                                 "props":{"name": format!("Person {i}"), "team": team}}]}}})
        })
        .collect();
    // recall, in ordinary words.
    calls.push(serde_json::json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":20,"method":"tools/call","params":{
        "name":"recall","arguments":{"topic":"who joined the infra team"}}}));
    // suggest_rules, as data, so the proposal's arguments can be passed on.
    calls.push(serde_json::json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":21,"method":"tools/call","params":{
        "name":"suggest_rules","arguments":{"json":true}}}));
    let r = mcp(&dir, &calls);

    for id in 1..=12u64 {
        assert!(!is_error(&r[&id]), "remember {id} failed: {}", text_of(&r[&id]));
    }
    let recalled = text_of(&r[&20]);
    assert!(
        recalled.contains("note:") && recalled.contains("infra"),
        "recall did not return a remembered fact: {recalled}"
    );

    let report: Js = serde_json::from_str(&text_of(&r[&21])).expect("suggest_rules json");
    let over_team = report["suggestions"]
        .as_array()
        .expect("suggestions")
        .iter()
        .find(|s| s["create_rule_args"].to_string().contains("\"field\":\"team\""))
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("no proposal over `team` on a twelve-person store: {report}"));
    let args = over_team["create_rule_args"].clone();
    let rule_name = args["name"].as_str().expect("a rule name").to_string();

    // create_rule with the proposal's arguments, unchanged; then the question
    // the product exists for.
    let r = mcp(
        &dir,
        &[
            serde_json::json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"tools/call","params":{
                "name":"create_rule","arguments": args}}),
            serde_json::json!({"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":2,"method":"tools/call","params":{
                "name":"explain_association","arguments":{"a":"p0","b":"p2"}}}),
        ],
    );
    assert!(!is_error(&r[&1]), "create_rule refused its own suggestion: {}", text_of(&r[&1]));
    let why = text_of(&r[&2]);
    assert!(
        why.contains(&rule_name) && why.contains("team"),
        "explain_association did not name the rule and the field: {why}"
    );
}
```

`mcp`, `text_of`, `is_error`, `tmp` and the alias `Js` are this file's own (`:24-96`). `p0` and `p2` are both on `infra`.

```bash
cargo test -p mushroomdb-cli --test first_run the_readme_quick_start 2>&1 | grep -E "^test |panicked|test result" 
```

**This test is not expected to be RED for a missing implementation — every tool it calls exists.** It is a claim being checked, and there are two honest outcomes:

- **It passes.** The README prints the four-step flow (Step 3's text as written). To see the test can fail, change `"field\":\"team\""` to `"field\":\"squad\""` and run it: `no proposal over \`team\`…` with the report printed. Change it back.
- **It fails at `no proposal over team`.** Raise the count from 12 in steps (24, 48) and re-run. If a count under 50 passes, use it, and say that number in the README sentence instead of "a dozen". If none does, `suggest_rules` is not a first-session tool: Step 3 then prints `remember → recall → create_rule → explain_association`, with `suggest_rules` named in prose as what proposes a rule once a store is large, and this test's middle section is replaced by a `create_rule` call with a hand-written `FieldEqual` rule on `team`. Report which outcome you got; it changes one code block and one sentence of the README and nothing else.

- [ ] **Step 3: The quick start**

Replace `README.md:45-51` (the paragraph beginning "One command writes" and the fenced flow) with:

````markdown
One command writes the `/mushroom` skill, an MCP server listing the twenty-three-tool association
surface, and the session hooks. Then the loop a session runs:

```text
remember  →  recall  →  suggest_rules  →  explain_association
 (a fact)    (find it)   (the store        (why two things
                          proposes a rule)   are related)
```

`remember` takes a sentence and the entities it names; a subject it has never seen is stubbed
rather than refused. `recall` answers a question in ordinary words. Once a dozen entities share a
value, `suggest_rules` proposes the rule that links them — nothing is created until you approve
its `create_rule` — and from then on every write maintains those edges. `explain_association`
says which rule linked two entities, and on what evidence.
````

The "Recall has three modes" bullet (`:182-186`) — `recall` is the natural-language path and is missing from its own list:

```markdown
- Recall has four modes: `recall` for a question in ordinary words, ranked over every indexed
  text field; `find_similar` by query vector (HNSW when available, brute force otherwise);
  `find_similar` by key (neighbors along a rule-derived edge type); and `query` for structured
  Cypher. `hybrid_search` fuses fulltext and vector results via Reciprocal Rank Fusion, and
  `pairwise_similar` answers "which of these are most like each other" over a caller's own key
  set, exactly — it never uses HNSW. **`find_similar`'s `min` defaults to 0.8 on every surface.**
```

The "Schema-as-code" bullet (`:200-201`):

```markdown
- Schema-as-code: `mushroomdb schema apply <dir> <schema.json>` idempotently applies rules, views,
  and fulltext indexes, printing a created/updated/unchanged diff. Two presets need no file:
  `--memory-defaults` gives an existing store the text fields `recall` searches, and
  `--memory-identity` adds the `SAME_AS` rules that link two keys for one entity, after saying
  what it will backfill.
```

The "**Minimal workflow** (four tool calls)" paragraph and its fenced flow (`:203-208`) — delete both. The quick start, now directly above, is the workflow; printing a second, different one is how the README came to describe two products.

````markdown
## Install

Pick the row for what you want to do. The paths are not interchangeable: the last column is what
each one leaves out.

| You want to… | Run | You get | You do not get |
|---|---|---|---|
| Give Claude Code a memory | `claude plugin marketplace add MatthewSherlin/mushroomdb` then `claude plugin install mushroom@mushroomdb` | The `/mushroom:mushroom` skill, the MCP server and its 23 tools, the session and prompt hooks | A store path of your choosing: it uses `./mushroom-memory` |
| The same in Cursor or Codex, or with a store you name | `npx mushroomdb install --db ./memory` | The `/mushroom` skill, the MCP entry, the hooks; `--platform claude-code\|cursor\|codex\|all` | Anything installed globally: the entry runs `npx` |
| See the graph in a browser | `npx mushroomdb demo ./db && npx mushroomdb serve ./db`, or `docker run --rm -p 8080:8080 -e MUSHROOMDB_TOKEN=changeme ghcr.io/matthewsherlin/mushroomdb` | The explorer UI and the HTTP API at `:8080` | An assistant wired to it: that is one of the two rows above |
| Use it from a shell only, with no MCP server | `npx mushroomdb install --delivery cli` | The skill, the hooks, and three commands: `why` (why two keys are related), `asof --at <date>` (a Cypher read at a past date), `query` | The other three headline questions as one call: no `edges_at`, `what_if` or `node_edges`, and no `remember` — a fact is a `CREATE` |
| Embed it in a Rust program | `cargo add mushroomdb` | The engine as a library | The CLI, the MCP server, the UI |
| Embed it in a Python program | `pip install mushroomdb` | The engine as a module: rules, Cypher, vectors, history, scoped handles, the graph algorithms, full-text, and the memory calls (`remember`, `recall`, `upsert_entity`, `forget`) as data | The MCP server and the UI: it is a library, not a tool surface |
| Build the CLI from crates.io | `cargo install mushroomdb-cli` | The `mushroomdb` binary | **The explorer UI.** This build has none, and `serve` answers the API only without saying so. Use the `npx` or Docker row to see the graph |

Docker details, `install.sh`, and building the binary with the UI embedded are in
[CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md).
````

**Files:**
- Create: `scripts/check-links.py`
- Modify: `scripts/gen-llms-full.sh` (`--check`, and a missing page is an error), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (two steps after the names-gate step)
- Move: `docs/site/launch-checklist.md` → `docs/roadmap/launch-checklist.md`; `docs/site/roadmap-moat.md` → `docs/roadmap/roadmap-moat.md`
- Modify: `docs/site/quickstart.md` (`:69-86`, `:156`, `:342-359`, `:400-402`, `:428`), `docs/site/index.md` (`:47-48`, `:57-64`, `:68-80`), `docs/site/api.md` (`:1012`, `:1019-1039`, `:1057-1058`, `:1081`), `docs/site/mcp.md` (`:429-430`, `:447`), `docs/site/ingest-git.md:1`, `CONTRIBUTING.md:62`, `docs/design.md` (a header; its last section)
- Modify: `docs/roadmap/v0.6.10-defects.md` (row 60)
- Regenerate: `llms-full.txt`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the README Task 13 left (the link gate reads it).
- Produces: `python3 scripts/check-links.py` — exit 0 when every relative link and `#anchor` in the hand-written docs resolves to a tracked file and a real heading, and every page in `docs/site/` is linked from `docs/site/index.md`; `bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh --check` — exit 0 when `llms-full.txt` is what the generator would write. Both run in CI. Tasks 15 and 16 run both.

**Why:** Spec §6.3 and R5. Five things, in the order they are done.

1. **Two gates first.** This task edits a dozen pages and moves two; every later task edits more. Nothing checks a relative link or an anchor today (one is already broken, `docs/site/api.md:1081`), and `llms-full.txt` — a generated copy of the README and eighteen pages that an assistant reads instead of them — is regenerated only by a version bump and checked by nothing. The gates are written and shown failing before the edits, so the edits are made under them.
2. **Corrections where a page contradicts the code.** `quickstart.md` teaches the commit-hunting `edges_at` was built to remove; `api.md` says "Seventeen tools"; three pages still describe a time before the first release.
3. **Internal notes out of the published directory.** `pages.yml` copies every file in `docs/site/` to the public site (`:35`).
4. **`docs/design.md`, marked as what it is** (S9).
5. **A complete index.** `docs/site/index.md` links 11 of the pages beside it.

**The `design.md` choice, argued.** R5 offers two options: correct it, or mark it clearly as a pre-implementation historical document; choose the smaller honest one. Correcting it is a rewrite — the storage model, the concurrency model, the testing section, the performance targets, the open items and eleven dead pointers are all wrong, which is most of the document — and the result would be a second architecture description beside `README.md#architecture` and `docs/format-stability.md`, free to drift from both. Marking it is twenty lines and is true forever: it *is* the design the project started from, and the two places the cross-binding conformance corpus and the 10M target are promised get an inline correction so nobody reads a promise as a fact. It is marked.

**Not done here, and said so:** the rendered site (PD-5), and the four pages spec §6.3 calls missing — the UI, error classes, namespaces, `on_conflict`. R5 does not list them; they are new writing, not correction, and belong with the site that would give them a reader. The one fact a UI page would have to state — which install paths have the UI — is in the README's install table as of Task 13.

- [ ] **Step 1: The link gate**

Create `scripts/check-links.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""check-links.py — every relative link and anchor in the hand-written docs resolves.

Checked, in each tracked Markdown file under the paths in SOURCES:

  - a relative link or image target exists and is tracked by git;
  - a `#fragment` on a link to a Markdown file matches a heading in that file,
    by the slug GitHub gives it;
  - every page in docs/site/ is linked from docs/site/index.md, so no page is
    reachable from nowhere a reader starts.

External URLs (http, https, mailto) are not fetched: a gate must not need a
network. Links inside fenced code blocks are ignored.

Exits 1 printing `KIND  file:line: target` for every problem.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

SOURCES = [
    "README.md",
    "CONTRIBUTING.md",
    "SECURITY.md",
    "llms.txt",
    "docs/site/*.md",
    "docs/design.md",
    "docs/format-stability.md",
    "docs/concurrency-decision.md",
    "docs/dogfood-report.md",
    "bindings/python/README.md",
    "clients/typescript/README.md",
    "packaging/plugin/README.md",
    "benchmarks/README.md",
]

# Pages in docs/site that are deliberately not in the index.
UNINDEXED = {"index.md"}

LINK = re.compile(
    r'(?<!\!)\[[^\]]*\]\(([^)\s]+)(?:\s+"[^"]*")?\)'  # [text](target "title")
    r'|\!\[[^\]]*\]\(([^)\s]+)\)'                     # ![alt](target)
    r'|<img[^>]+src="([^"]+)"'                        # <img src="target">
)


def git(*args: str) -> str:
    return subprocess.check_output(["git", "-C", str(ROOT), *args], text=True)


def slug(heading: str) -> str:
    """The anchor GitHub generates for a heading."""
    h = heading.strip().lower()
    h = re.sub(r"\[([^\]]*)\]\([^)]*\)", r"\1", h)  # a link in a heading keeps its text
    h = h.replace("`", "")
    h = re.sub(r"[^\w\- ]", "", h, flags=re.UNICODE)
    return h.replace(" ", "-")


def anchors(path: Path) -> set[str]:
    out: set[str] = set()
    seen: dict[str, int] = {}
    fenced = False
    for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        if line.lstrip().startswith("```"):
            fenced = not fenced
        if fenced:
            continue
        m = re.match(r"^(#{1,6})\s+(.*?)\s*#*\s*$", line)
        if m:
            s = slug(m.group(2))
            n = seen.get(s, 0)
            seen[s] = n + 1
            out.add(s if n == 0 else f"{s}-{n}")
        out.update(re.findall(r'<a\s+(?:name|id)="([^"]+)"', line))
    return out


def main() -> int:
    tracked = set(git("ls-files").splitlines())
    files = [f for f in git("ls-files", "--", *SOURCES).splitlines() if f]
    problems: list[str] = []
    index_targets: set[str] = set()

    for rel in files:
        path = ROOT / rel
        fenced = False
        for lineno, line in enumerate(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines(), 1):
            if line.lstrip().startswith("```"):
                fenced = not fenced
            if fenced:
                continue
            for m in LINK.finditer(line):
                target = next(g for g in m.groups() if g)
                if re.match(r"^(https?:|mailto:)", target):
                    continue
                file_part, _, fragment = target.partition("#")
                dest = (path.parent / file_part).resolve() if file_part else path
                try:
                    dest_rel = str(dest.relative_to(ROOT))
                except ValueError:
                    problems.append(f"OUTSIDE   {rel}:{lineno}: {target}")
                    continue
                if not dest.exists():
                    problems.append(f"MISSING   {rel}:{lineno}: {target}")
                    continue
                if dest.is_file() and dest_rel not in tracked:
                    problems.append(f"UNTRACKED {rel}:{lineno}: {target}")
                if fragment and dest.suffix == ".md" and fragment not in anchors(dest):
                    problems.append(f"ANCHOR    {rel}:{lineno}: {target}")
                if rel == "docs/site/index.md":
                    index_targets.add(dest_rel)

    for page in sorted(f for f in tracked if re.fullmatch(r"docs/site/[^/]+\.md", f)):
        if Path(page).name in UNINDEXED:
            continue
        if page not in index_targets:
            problems.append(f"UNINDEXED {page}: not linked from docs/site/index.md")

    for p in problems:
        print(p)
    if problems:
        print(f"check-links.py: FAILED — {len(problems)} problem(s) in {len(files)} files", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1
    print(f"check-links.py: OK — {len(files)} files")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
```

```bash
python3 scripts/check-links.py; echo "exit=$?"
```

Expected RED, on the tree Task 13 left: `ANCHOR    docs/site/api.md:1081: #concurrency-1`; one `UNINDEXED` line for each of the fourteen pages `index.md` does not link — `algorithms`, `deployment`, `durability`, `fulltext`, `indexes`, `launch-checklist`, `multiprocess`, `query`, `roadmap-moat`, `subscriptions`, `suggest`, `testing`, `timetravel`, `views`; and `exit=1`. If it also prints a `MISSING` line, that is a real broken link this plan's research did not find: fix it in Step 4 and name it in your report. If it prints an `UNTRACKED` line for a path under `benchmarks/agent-tasks/results/`, that directory's `.gitignore` rule keeps only `summary*.md` — a link to anything else there is broken for every reader but you.

- [ ] **Step 2: The generated copy gets a check mode**

Replace `scripts/gen-llms-full.sh` from its `set -euo pipefail` line (`:9`) to the end with:

```bash
set -euo pipefail

usage() { echo "usage: gen-llms-full.sh [--check]" >&2; exit 2; }
CHECK=0
case "${1:-}" in
    "") ;;
    --check) CHECK=1 ;;
    *) usage ;;
esac
[[ $# -le 1 ]] || usage

REPO_ROOT="$(git -C "$(dirname "$0")" rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
OUT="$REPO_ROOT/llms-full.txt"

SITE_DIR="$REPO_ROOT/docs/site"
README="$REPO_ROOT/README.md"

# Ordered list of site docs — index first, then feature docs, then reference
SITE_DOCS=(
    index.md
    quickstart.md
    concurrency.md
    multiprocess.md
    rules.md
    mcp.md
    association-bench.md
    query.md
    api.md
    subscriptions.md
    timetravel.md
    views.md
    fulltext.md
    algorithms.md
    masks.md
    service.md
    suggest.md
    testing.md
)

# A page named here that is not on disk used to be skipped without a word, so
# a typo — or a page moved out of docs/site — silently shortened the output.
for doc in "${SITE_DOCS[@]}"; do
    if [[ ! -f "$SITE_DIR/$doc" ]]; then
        echo "gen-llms-full.sh: SITE_DOCS lists $doc, which is not in docs/site" >&2
        exit 1
    fi
done

# Strip raw HTML tags, keeping every other angle-bracket token intact.
#
# A blanket `s/<[^>]*>//g` cannot be used here: the sources are full of tokens
# that look like tags and are not. CLI placeholders (`<db-dir>`, `<path>`,
# `<dest>`), Rust generics (`Vec<String>`, `Option<f64>`), the GraphML `<data>`
# element, and the map digest's literal `<mixed>` cluster label would all be
# deleted silently, corrupting pasted command lines and real tool output.
#
# So only a known list of HTML tag names is stripped. Three of those names
# collide with real content — `mushroomdb why <db> <a> <b>` writes `<a>` and
# `<b>` bare — so those are stripped only in a form markup actually takes: a
# closing tag, or an opening tag carrying an attribute.
HTML_BLOCK_TAGS='img|br|hr|p|div|details|summary|picture|source|figure|figcaption|h[1-6]'
HTML_AMBIGUOUS_TAGS='a|b|i|em|strong|span|code|sub|sup'

strip_html() {
    sed -E \
        -e "s@</($HTML_BLOCK_TAGS|$HTML_AMBIGUOUS_TAGS)>@@g" \
        -e "s@<($HTML_BLOCK_TAGS|$HTML_AMBIGUOUS_TAGS)[[:space:]][^>]*>@@g" \
        -e "s@<($HTML_BLOCK_TAGS)[[:space:]]*/?>@@g" \
        "$1"
}

generate() {
    echo "# mushroomdb — full LLM reference"
    echo "# Generated by scripts/gen-llms-full.sh (mushroomdb repo)"
    echo "# Source: README.md + docs/site/*.md"
    echo "# This file is tracked; regenerate and commit with: bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh"
    echo ""
    echo "---"
    echo ""

    echo "## README"
    echo ""
    # Strip raw HTML tags; keep text, code blocks and literal <angle> tokens
    strip_html "$README"
    echo ""
    echo "---"
    echo ""

    for doc in "${SITE_DOCS[@]}"; do
        echo "## docs/site/$doc"
        echo ""
        # Same rules as the README above
        strip_html "$SITE_DIR/$doc"
        echo ""
        echo "---"
        echo ""
    done
}

if [[ $CHECK -eq 1 ]]; then
    TMP="$(mktemp)"
    trap 'rm -f "$TMP"' EXIT
    generate > "$TMP"
    if ! cmp -s "$TMP" "$OUT"; then
        echo "gen-llms-full.sh: llms-full.txt is not what the generator writes." >&2
        echo "  README.md or a page in docs/site changed without it. Run: bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh" >&2
        diff "$OUT" "$TMP" | head -20 >&2 || true
        exit 1
    fi
    echo "gen-llms-full.sh: OK — llms-full.txt is current"
    exit 0
fi

generate > "$OUT"

SIZE=$(wc -c < "$OUT")
LINES=$(wc -l < "$OUT")

echo "Generated: $OUT"
echo "  size:  $SIZE bytes"
echo "  lines: $LINES"
```

The header comment (`:1-8`) stays; add one line to it: `# To check it is current without writing: bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh --check`. The output is byte-identical to the old script's for the same inputs — the only behavioural change in the write path is that a missing page is now an error instead of a silent skip.

  && bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh --check \
  && cp README.md /tmp/README.probe && printf '\nprobe line\n' >> README.md \
  && { bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh --check; echo "exit=$?"; } ; cp /tmp/README.probe README.md && rm -f /tmp/README.probe \
  && bash scripts/gen-llms-full.sh --check

- [ ] **Step 4: The corrections**

**`docs/site/quickstart.md:69-86`** — the `edges_at` example teaches a commit index and then insists `at` is not a date. Replace the request block, the reply's header line and the paragraph under it:

````markdown
```json
// tool: edges_at
{ "key": "person-01", "at": "2026-06-19", "label": "Project" }

### Task 15: The changelog leads with what the release is for (R3, spec §5.1)

**Files:**
- Modify: `CHANGELOG.md:3-8` (the title and what follows it), and four insertions further down the 0.7.0 section
- Modify: `docs/roadmap/v0.7-memory-write-path-spec.md` (corrections C11–C16, in place)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: what Tasks 2–14 actually landed. **Read `git log --oneline ae4d6c6..HEAD` first** and delete from the text below every bullet whose task was cut.
- Produces: the 0.7.0 section Task 16 un-marks as unreleased.

**Why:** Controller R3; correction S13. The section is titled "the code graph door is closed" and opens with what was removed. The release exists because `remember` followed by `recall` returned nothing on 0.6.12 (spec §1), and plan 1 — which fixed that — never wrote an entry: the fix appears once, as an aside inside a bullet about the prompt hook (`:69-70`). And the one change that breaks a caller silently, `find_similar`'s `min`, is not mentioned in the section at all.

A reader gets three things from the top of a breaking release's notes, in this order: **what will change under me without telling me**, **what the release is for**, and **what was taken away**. The section is rebuilt in that order. Everything from `### Removed — the code-graph door` (`:9`) down is kept as written, with four additions.

- [ ] **Step 1: The title, the warning, and plan 1's entry**

Replace `CHANGELOG.md:3` — the heading — and insert the two blocks after the "first breaking release" paragraph (`:5-7`), before `### Removed — the code-graph door`:

```markdown
## v0.7.0 (unreleased) — memory that fills itself
```

```markdown
> **If you call `find_similar` without `min`, your results change, and nothing tells you.**
>
> `min` now defaults to **0.8 on every surface**. Over MCP it always did. In the **Python
> binding** and on **HTTP `POST /find_similar`** it was `0.0`: a call that never named `min`
> returned up to `k` hits at any similarity, and now returns only those scoring 0.8 or above —
> which can be none. No error, no warning, fewer results.
>
> **To keep the old behaviour, pass `min=0.0` (Python) or `"min": 0.0` (HTTP).**
>
> `pairwise_similar` is unchanged — `0.0` everywhere. This default was held back for the first
> breaking release twice, in 0.6.10 and 0.6.11; this is that release.

### The memory write path

On 0.6.12, `remember` followed by `recall` returned nothing. The store held the note and the
index held its words; `recall` discarded every topic that did not look like a code identifier.
0.7 is the release in which the first minute on a new store works, and it is built around that.

- **`recall` answers ordinary language.** "who is Matthew Sherlin?" finds the entity and the
  notes about it. The gate that required a path, a `mod::name`, a snake_case word or backticks
  is deleted; an identifier still works.
- **`recall` tells "no text index" from "no match".** A store that cannot search says so, with
  the command that fixes it, instead of answering as if the topic were unknown.
- **`remember` takes what a sentence names.** Beside `text` and `about` it accepts `entities` —
  each a key, a label, properties and aliases, created or described in the same commit — and
  `facts`, relationships among them. The reply says what it created, what it matched, and what
  it could only stub.
- **An unknown subject is stubbed, not refused.** `remember {about: ["matthew"]}` on a store
  that has never heard of `matthew` used to fail. It now creates a provisional `Entity`, marked
  as one, that the `schema` tool counts and lists. A provisional node never expires; describing
  it — `upsert_entity`, or `remember`'s `entities` — clears the mark, and `forget` removes it.
  At most 20 are stubbed per call; any past that are named in the reply as **not** created.
- **A node's label is fixed.** `upsert_entity` with a `label` that differs from the stored one
  is refused, and writes nothing, where it used to be silently ignored. Name the label in
  `remember`'s `entities` at first mention.
- **Every new store can be recalled from.** `mcp`, `serve` and `demo` create a store with the
  memory schema: full-text on `Note.text`, `Concept.summary` and the name of each entity label.
  `remember` declares the name field of a label it has not seen, up to 32 text fields in all.
- **An existing store is never upgraded behind your back.** Declaring full-text makes a store
  slower to open, so a 0.6 store gains `recall` when you ask:
  `mushroomdb schema apply <db> --memory-defaults`. Until then `recall` and the session brief
  say exactly that.
- **A topic is at most 24 search terms.** A pasted paragraph is cut to its first 24 content
  words rather than turned into a query the index must scan for every one of.
- **The first minute is a test.** `crates/cli/tests/first_run.rs` drives the real binary over
  stdio through the flows that failed on 0.6.12 — and the README's quick start, as printed.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: The binding's section**

Insert after the `### Added — identity, and four tools for the store as a whole` section's last bullet and before `### Fixed` (`:216`). Delete any bullet whose task was cut; if Tasks 6, 7 and 8 were all cut, omit the section.

```markdown
### Added — the Python binding reaches the memory surface

- **Graph algorithms**: `pagerank`, `connected_components`, `degree_centrality` and
  `communities`. `degree()` and `degrees()` are unchanged and are a different thing — the
  degree of the keys you name. `budget_ms` defaults to `0`, no time limit, so the same store
  gives the same answer.
- **Full-text**: `enable_fulltext` (with `if_not_exists=`), `disable_fulltext`,
  `is_fulltext_enabled`, `fulltext_pairs` and `search`.
- **The rule lifecycle**: `rules`, `delete_rule`, `rebuild_rule` and `suggest_rules`, whose
  `create_rule_args` create the same rule here as they do over MCP.
- **The memory calls, as data**: `remember`, `recall` (ranked rows, not a digest),
  `upsert_entity` (aliases maintained, a provisional mark cleared, no relabel), `schema_report`,
  `forget` and `identity_clusters`. Each calls the function the MCP tool calls.
- **`set_props_many`** sets properties on many existing nodes in one commit. Unchanged values
  write nothing, an unknown key is an error and nothing is written, and rules fire inside the
  commit. `wal_total_commits()` moves by one frame, or by two when a rule derived or retracted
  an edge — never by the number of nodes.
- **Whole-store reads are refused on a `scoped()` handle.** The algorithms, `search`,
  `fulltext_pairs`, `rules`, `suggest_rules`, `recall`, `schema_report` and `identity_clusters`
  take no mask, so each raises `ValueError` there rather than answer over nodes the scope hides.
```

The ignored-test census is deliberately not among them: it prints and cannot fail (ledger row 46), and a changelog that called it a gate would be claiming a guard that guards nothing. ### Task 16: Prepare the release — bump, rehearse, gate, and stop (PD-6)

**Files:**
- Modify: `scripts/bump-version.sh` (lockfiles in `--check`; a failed refresh is a failure; `scripts/` out of the pin rewrite; the header)
- Modify: `scripts/check-claims.sh` (one pattern)
- Create: `scripts/publish-rehearsal.sh`
- Modify, by the bump: `Cargo.toml`, `crates/cli/Cargo.toml`, `crates/core-bench/Cargo.toml`, `crates/sim-harness/Cargo.toml`, `bindings/python/Cargo.toml`, the sixteen inter-crate pins in seven manifests, `bindings/python/pyproject.toml`, `packaging/npm/package.json`, `clients/typescript/package.json`, `.well-known/mcp/server-card.json`, `server.json`, `docs/site/mcp.md:30`, `llms.txt:276`, `Cargo.lock`, `bindings/python/Cargo.lock`, `packaging/plugin/**`, `llms-full.txt`
- Modify: `CHANGELOG.md:3` (the heading), `docs/roadmap/v0.6.10-defects.md` (rows 58, 59)
- Create: `docs/roadmap/v0.7-release-checklist.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything.
- Produces: a branch at version 0.7.0 with every gate green locally, a rehearsed publish, and `docs/roadmap/v0.7-release-checklist.md` — the steps only the owner takes.

**Why:** Spec task 18, cut in two (S14, PD-6). The agent half is everything that can be undone: the bump, the gates, a rehearsal. The human half is everything that cannot.

**What the tag does, so nobody treats it as a step.** `release.yml` runs on any pushed `v*` tag (`:8-10`): three binaries, a GitHub Release (published at `:146`), two GHCR tags including `:latest` (`:163-170`, in parallel with the binaries and waiting for nothing), and a dispatch of `publish.yml` (`:187`), which publishes eight crates in dependency order (`:113-115`), three wheels to PyPI and two npm packages. Eight crates.io versions and one PyPI version can never be republished; an npm version number is burned once used. **No step below pushes, tags, merges, publishes, or dispatches a workflow.**

**Three defects in the bump itself, fixed before it runs** (ledger row 58). They are the failure shape spec §7.1 was written to close — a check that cannot see what it guards — one layer out:

2. A release named in prose is invisible to every scan. `docs/site/index.md:57` was that sentence; Task 14 reworded it to name no version. A pattern keeps it from coming back.
3. The pin rewrite (`:127-141`) covers tracked `*.sh`, so a real bump rewrites the script's own explanatory comment at `:135`.

Replace the header's first line and the paragraph that counts (`:2-8`) so it names no number that goes stale:

```bash
# One version, many places.
#
# The workspace version lives in `Cargo.toml`, and nothing else inherits it:
# several crates carry a literal because `cargo package` needs one, every
# inter-crate dependency pin carries it again so a published crate can resolve
# its siblings, the package manifests outside Cargo carry it for npm, PyPI, the
# MCP registry and the discovery card, and two lockfiles record it.
```

After step 4 (`pyproject.toml`, ending at `:110`) and before the `if [[ $CHECK -eq 1 ]]` block (`:112`), add:

```bash
# 5. Lockfiles. `--check` read neither until 0.7, and the binding's refresh
#    below swallowed its own failure, so a lockfile naming the previous version
#    for this workspace's own packages passed. Checked, never edited: a bump
#    regenerates them with `cargo metadata`.
if [[ $CHECK -eq 1 ]]; then
  for lock in Cargo.lock bindings/python/Cargo.lock; do
    while IFS= read -r cur; do
      [[ "$cur" == "$NEW" ]] && continue
      report "$lock" "a workspace package entry" "$cur"
    done < <(awk '
      /^name = "(mushroomdb|mushroomdb-[a-z]+|core-bench|sim-harness)"$/ { want = 1; next }
      want && /^version = "/ { gsub(/^version = "|"$/, ""); print; want = 0; next }
      { want = 0 }
    ' "$lock")
  done
fi
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Rehearse the publish**

Create `scripts/publish-rehearsal.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# publish-rehearsal.sh — package everything a `v*` tag publishes, and upload nothing.
#
# The first time the eight crates were ever packaged at a new version used to
# be the real `cargo publish`, in dependency order, each one irreversible. A
# mis-pinned sibling or a file missing from a package would be found after
# three crates were already on the index. This finds it before.
#
#   publish-rehearsal.sh           crates (verified build), wheel, both npm packages
#   publish-rehearsal.sh --quick   crates without the verify build (the wheel and npm steps still run)
#
# Every command here is a dry run or writes only build output and a temp
# directory. Nothing contacts a registry to upload. There is no flag that
# makes this script publish; publishing is a tag, and a person pushes it.
set -euo pipefail
ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
cd "$ROOT"

QUICK=0
case "${1:-}" in
  "") ;;
  --quick) QUICK=1 ;;
  *) echo "usage: publish-rehearsal.sh [--quick]" >&2; exit 2 ;;
esac

WS="$(awk -F'"' '/^version = "/{print $2; exit}' Cargo.toml)"
OUT="$(mktemp -d)"
trap 'rm -rf "$OUT"' EXIT
fail=0
step() { printf '\n── %s ──\n' "$1"; }
bad()  { echo "publish-rehearsal.sh: FAILED — $1" >&2; fail=1; }

step "version sites agree on $WS"
bash scripts/bump-version.sh --check || bad "version sites disagree"
# Nothing below means anything on manifests that disagree, and all of it is
# slow: a release wheel build, two npm builds. Stop here.
if [[ $fail -ne 0 ]]; then
  echo "publish-rehearsal.sh: FAILED — fix the version sites first. Nothing was packaged or uploaded." >&2
  exit 1
fi

# The order publish.yml uses (.github/workflows/publish.yml, the crates.io job).
CRATES=(mushroomdb-storage mushroomdb-rules mushroomdb-query mushroomdb
        mushroomdb-arrow mushroomdb-server mushroomdb-extract mushroomdb-cli)

step "crates: package all eight together, siblings resolved from the workspace"
args=(publish --dry-run --workspace --exclude core-bench --exclude sim-harness --allow-dirty)
[[ $QUICK -eq 1 ]] && args+=(--no-verify)
if ! cargo "${args[@]}"; then
  bad "cargo publish --dry-run --workspace"
fi
for c in "${CRATES[@]}"; do
  if ! ls "target/package/${c}-${WS}.crate" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    bad "no packaged tarball for ${c} ${WS}"
  fi
done

step "wheel: build as publish.yml does, and look inside"
if [[ -x bindings/python/.venv/bin/maturin ]]; then
  if (cd bindings/python && .venv/bin/maturin build --release --out "$OUT/wheel" >/dev/null); then
    wheel="$(ls "$OUT"/wheel/mushroomdb-"${WS}"-*abi3*.whl 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
    if [[ -z "$wheel" ]]; then
      bad "no abi3 wheel named mushroomdb-${WS}-…"
    else
      echo "built $(basename "$wheel")"
      listing="$(python3 -m zipfile -l "$wheel")"
      for want in "mushroomdb/__init__.pyi" "mushroomdb/py.typed"; do
        echo "$listing" | grep -q "$want" || bad "wheel is missing $want"
      done
    fi
  else
    bad "maturin build"
  fi
else
  echo "skipped: no bindings/python/.venv/bin/maturin"
fi

step "npm: pack both packages without publishing"
if command -v npm >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  for dir in packaging/npm clients/typescript; do
    name="$(node -p "require('./$dir/package.json').name")"
    ver="$(node -p "require('./$dir/package.json').version")"
    [[ "$ver" == "$WS" ]] || bad "$dir/package.json is $ver, workspace is $WS"
    if [[ "$dir" == clients/typescript ]]; then
      (cd "$dir" && npm ci >/dev/null 2>&1 && npm run build >/dev/null 2>&1) || bad "$name: build"
    fi
    (cd "$dir" && npm pack --dry-run >/dev/null 2>&1) && echo "packed $name@$ver (dry run)" \
      || bad "$name: npm pack --dry-run"
  done
else
  echo "skipped: npm is not installed"
fi

echo
if [[ $fail -ne 0 ]]; then
  echo "publish-rehearsal.sh: FAILED — see above. Nothing was uploaded." >&2
  exit 1
fi
echo "publish-rehearsal.sh: OK — $WS packages cleanly. Nothing was uploaded."
```

Show it can fail before trusting it. The probe breaks one pin, and the script stops at its first step — the version-site check — before any packaging, wheel build or npm build runs, so the probe costs seconds. One invocation, restoring from a copy:

```bash
set -o pipefail; cd /Users/nilrehsttam/Repos/graph-db && cp crates/server/Cargo.toml /tmp/server.probe \
  && perl -0pi -e 's/(path = "\.\.\/core-api", version = ")[^"]*(")/${1}0.6.12${2}/' crates/server/Cargo.toml \
  && { bash scripts/publish-rehearsal.sh --quick; echo "exit=$?"; } ; cp /tmp/server.probe crates/server/Cargo.toml && rm -f /tmp/server.probe
```

Expected RED: `bump-version.sh: crates/server/Cargo.toml — an inter-crate pin is 0.6.12, workspace is 0.7.0`, then `publish-rehearsal.sh: FAILED — fix the version sites first. Nothing was packaged or uploaded.`, `exit=1`, and no `── crates` section after it. This is the failure the script's header describes — a sibling pinned to the previous minor, which resolved under caret for every 0.6.x and does not for 0.7.0.

Then the real rehearsal, behind the disk check — the verified form builds each crate from its packaged tarball under `target/package`:

```bash

  && df -g / | awk 'NR==2 {print "free_gib=" $4; if ($4 < 15) {print "BLOCKED: under 15 GiB free"; exit 1}}' \

```

**This script was not run when the plan was written, and one behaviour is unverified:** that `cargo publish --dry-run --workspace` on the pinned 1.92 toolchain resolves the eight crates' dependencies on each other from the workspace when 0.7.0 is on no registry. The flags exist (`cargo publish --help` lists `--workspace`, `--dry-run`, `--exclude` and `--no-verify`). If it instead fails to find `mushroomdb-storage 0.7.0` on crates.io, the rehearsal cannot verify crates past the first and the script needs the weaker form: replace the single `cargo publish` call with a loop over `CRATES` running `cargo package -p "$c" --no-verify --allow-dirty`, keep the tarball check, and **say in the checklist's first line that the crates were packaged but not verified against each other**. If disk is short, `--quick` is the rehearsal; say that too. Report which form ran.

Prepared by plan 4, Task 16. The branch `feat/v0.7-memory-write-path` is at `<head sha>`,
version 0.7.0, with every local gate green: `<N binaries, P passed, 0 failed, I ignored>`, both

against each other | --quick>`).

**Nothing here has been done.** No push, no pull request, no tag. Each step below is yours.

## Before anything leaves this machine

- [ ] Read plan 4's "Decisions taken on the owner's behalf" (PD-1 to PD-6) and "Owner decisions
      still open" (Q1 to Q5). A tag freezes them. The two that change what a user sees:
      **PD-1**, `find_similar`'s `min` is 0.8 everywhere, and **PD-2**, the repository names no
      other system.
- [ ] Read `CHANGELOG.md`'s 0.7.0 section top to bottom. Its first block is the `min` warning.
- [ ] Decide Q2 — the one comparison nobody ran (`ingest` against `insert_node`, ledger row 47):
      before the tag, or in 0.7.1.

## The pull request

- [ ] `git push -u origin feat/v0.7-memory-write-path`
- [ ] Open the pull request against `main`.
- [ ] **Wait for CI. This is its first run on this branch** — every green so far is local, on
      macOS. The jobs: `test`, `recall-gates`, `python`, `mcp-handshake`, `plugin-validate` and
      the rest of `ci.yml`. Budget for red: a Linux-only failure is likely somewhere in ninety
      commits. Fix on the branch; do not merge red.
- [ ] Confirm `plugin-validate` ran the gates this release added: the names gate's own test,
      `check-links.py`, `gen-llms-full.sh --check`.

## The merge

- [ ] Merge. **Merging publishes the docs**: `pages.yml` deploys `docs/site/` on every push to
      `main` that touches it, so the site describes 0.7 from this moment, while every registry
      still serves 0.6.12. Tag soon after, or accept the window.
- [ ] Wait for CI on `main` to finish green. `release.yml`'s `docker` job pushes `:latest`
      without waiting for any test (ledger row 61), so the tag must go on a commit that is
      already known green.

## The tag — nothing after this can be taken back

- [ ] `git tag v0.7.0 <the merge commit>` and `git push origin v0.7.0`.

What that starts, in `release.yml` then `publish.yml`:

| What | Where | Can it be undone? |
|---|---|---|
| Three binaries, `SHA256SUMS`, the Homebrew formula | the GitHub Release | Deletable — but `install.sh` and the formula resolve through it |
| `:latest` and `:v0.7.0` | `ghcr.io/…/mushroomdb` | Overwritable; pulled images are out |
| Eight crates, in order, 30 s apart | crates.io | **Never.** Yank only; `0.7.0` cannot be republished |
| Three abi3 wheels | PyPI | **Never** for that version number |
| `mushroomdb` and `mushroomdb-client` | npm | The number is burned once used |

- [ ] Watch `release.yml`: `binaries` (three), `github-release`, `docker`, `trigger-publish`.
- [ ] Watch `publish.yml`: `crates.io`, `PyPI` and `PyPI upload`, both `npm` jobs, `npx smoke test`.
- [ ] **If the crates job fails part-way, re-run `publish.yml` on the tag.** It skips crates
      already on the index. Do not delete the tag, and do not re-tag: a second tag cannot
      republish the crates the first one landed.
- [ ] `smoke-npx` runs after the npm publish. It detects a broken release; it cannot prevent one.

## After

- [ ] `npx -y mushroomdb@0.7.0 --version` prints `0.7.0`.
- [ ] `pip install mushroomdb==0.7.0` in a fresh virtualenv, then
      `python -c "import mushroomdb; print(mushroomdb.GraphDb.find_similar.__text_signature__)"`
      shows `min=0.8`.
- [ ] `cargo install mushroomdb-cli --version 0.7.0` builds.
- [ ] The GitHub Release's notes are generated from commit titles; replace them with the
      changelog's 0.7.0 section, starting at the `min` warning.

## Known costs this plan accepts

- **Python and HTTP callers of `find_similar` that omit `min` get fewer results, silently.** That is PD-1's whole cost and the changelog's first block. `find_similar` (0.8) and `pairwise_similar` (0.0) now disagree on every surface, where they used to disagree only on MCP.
- **None of this plan's code was compiled before it was written.** The first GREEN run of each task is its first compile. The self-review lists what was read rather than run.
- **The names gate does not see a name split by a space or a hyphen, cannot hold a name that is an ordinary word, and is not secret.** A one-time grep covers the first at scrub time and hand edits the second; a dictionary recovers the list.
- **Three benchmark adapters and their skip-path tests are deleted**, and with them the ability to re-run the old cross-engine comparison from this tree. Git history keeps them.
- **Historical records are edited.** Old plans, committed results and one released note have names replaced by placeholders, and two have a dated correction added. Their numbers and decisions are untouched.
- **Eleven binding methods raise `ValueError` on a `scoped()` handle.** A sidecar that narrows its handle and then wants PageRank must call it on the unscoped handle. Refusing is the only sound answer the engine supports today.
- **The `props["ns"]`-against-`namespace=` rule is stated in three places**: the server's `stamp_namespace_row`, the binding's `insert_node`, and now the binding's `upsert_entity`, each in its own words. R1 says shared policy belongs in `core-api`; this one was left where it is because moving it means changing `insert_node` and the MCP ingest path too. It is listed under "Not in this plan" for 0.7.1.
- **The binding's errors for memory refusals keep the `ingest error: ` prefix** in `str(e)`; `.detail` is the bare sentence. MCP strips the prefix; changing `graph_err` would change every existing `IngestError` message.
- **`remember`'s report key is `note` in Python and `key` over MCP.** The binding returns the report's own field names.
- **A store the binding creates has no memory schema**, and the identity preset is a CLI step.
- **`suggest_rules` in Python is uncapped** where MCP shows five; and it still spends the engine's 5-second budget profiling bookkeeping fields before dropping them.
- **`set_props_many` reads each property once before writing**, under the lock. For a row that changes everything that is one wasted read per property; it is what makes an unchanged value free.
- **`schema`'s JSON report and `explain_association`'s JSON report stay uncapped.** Only the text is bounded.
- **`docs/design.md` stays wrong in its body.** It is marked historical, and two promises get an inline correction; the rest is the record of an intent.
- **The hook-latency number is one machine, warm cache, release build.** A cold cache and a store with the identity preset are not measured.
- **The front-page numbers are one run on one machine.** If Task 11 Step 10 is cut, they are v0.1.1's, dated.
- **The publish rehearsal's central command was not run when the plan was written**, and has a weaker fallback.
- **CI has never run on this branch.** Every gate in Task 16 is a local macOS result.
- **Merging publishes the docs before the registries have 0.7.0.** The checklist says to tag promptly.
- **Six new things run in CI's `plugin-validate` job**: the names gate through `check-claims.sh`, its test, the link gate, the generated-docs check, a retired-command rule and two claim patterns. Each can fail a pull request that was green before.

PD-1 to PD-6 above are taken and flagged; each is restated here with its options, then the questions this plan found and could only default.

| | Taken as | The other option | What changes |
|---|---|---|---|

| PD-4 | **Restate**; no large run | Run 1,000,000 nodes and state that | A new measurement task |

### Q1 — the deviation on `analyze` (DV-1)

- **(a) Do not relocate it** — as planned. No binding method consumes it.
- **(b) Relocate it anyway**, for a later HTTP route or a Python `analyze`. One more task of Task 3's shape.

### Q2 — the comparison nobody ran (ledger row 47)

Plan 1 moved `upsert_entity`'s create path from `ingest` to `insert_node` and its handoff says "worth one explicit check before release". No ledger records it.

- **(a) After the tag** — as planned; the row is deferred to 0.7.1.
- **(b) Before the tag** — one test that creates the same entity both ways and compares properties, list handling and index lookups. Half a day; it could find a real difference.

### Q3 — the memory schema on the binding

- **(a) CLI only** — as planned. A Python store gets the identity preset from `mushroomdb schema apply`.
- **(b) Add `apply_memory_schema(preset)` to the binding.** Small; it also answers ledger row 55's half about a binding-created store having no schema.

### Q4 — how `rules()` and `fulltext_pairs()` behave on a scoped handle

- **(a) Refused** — as planned (R1). They enumerate labels a scope may hide.
- **(b) Answered unscoped**, as `has_vector_rule` and `is_index_enabled` are: schema is not node data. Two rows move in `test_scope_leaks.py`.

### Q5 — plan 2's D3, still unconfirmed

The owner chose to delete the code-suite benchmark files; plan 2 kept the six files and made `--suite code` refuse instead, because the association suite runs through them. Surfaced at plan 2's finish and never answered.

- **(a) Keep them** — as the tree is.
- **(b) Delete what the association suite does not use.** A task for 0.7.1.
